In this section, we empirically evaluate the scalability of our approach based on the programs from the previous sections, i.e., our case studies and the Monte-Carlo approximator from
Figure

1
.
For each of these programs and post-expectations, we verify the tightest upper bound (up to a precision of
3
decimal places) on the respective weakest pre-expectation for increasing values of
N
(except for
diverging
where
N=2
already yields the precise weakest pre-expectation).

Our empirical results are depicted in
Table

2
.
Column
Program
depicts the respective program,
\mathbf{N}
denotes the partition size for the Riemann pre-expectation,
Time
denotes the verification time in seconds (i.e., verification condition generation and time for SMT solving) required by
Caesar
,
|AST|
denotes the size of the formula offloaded to the SMT solver
Z3
(i.e., the number of nodes in the formula’s abstract syntax tree),
Post
denotes the post-expectation, and
Bound
depicts the upper (lower) bound on the (liberal) weakest pre-expectation. For the
diverging
benchmark, we lower-bound a liberal weakest pre-expectation.
All other benchmarks upper-bound a non-liberal weakest pre-expectation.
The bounds were inferred manually in a binary search-like fashion.

With our encoding of Riemann pre-expectations described in
Section

9.1
,
Caesar
allows verifying increasingly sharper bounds when increasing the partition size
N

The size of the generated formula for the verification conditions mostly increases moderately when increasing
N
.
This is to be expected since the formula size grows polynomially in
N
for a fixed program and a fixed post-expectation (see
Section

8.3.1
).
The time required for verification, however, can increase drastically when doubling the value of
N
(see entries of
Table

2
where
N=32
).

10.
Related Work

Integral Approximation.

Our approach relies on under- and over-approximation of Lebesgue integrals via Riemann sums, achieved by discretizing the domain of the continuous uniform distributions in the program.
Recently,
(
Garg et al., 2024
)
proposed an efficient
bit blasting
discretization method for continuous mixed-gamma distributions (which subsume the uniform distributions) for programs with bounded (
\mathtt{for}
) loops.
In
(
Beutner et al., 2022
)
, integral approximation is used to derive guaranteed bounds on a program’s posterior distribution.
Following up on this,
(
Wang et al., 2024
)
extended the computation of such bounds to a class of programs with soft conditioning (
score-at-end
programs).
Previously,
(
Sankaranarayanan et al., 2013
)
and
(
Albarghouthi et al., 2017
)
used integral approximations to bound the satisfaction probabilities of properties like safety and fairness.
However, these approaches are not able to deal with unbounded loops in full generality.
AQUA
(
Huang et al., 2022
)
uses a different discretization method, where the posterior of a given program is approximated by quantizing the state space.
However, this approximation does not necessarily provide an upper or lower bound of the true distribution, and unbounded loops are not supported.
Finally,
(
Wang et al., 2021a
)
propose a method to derive upper and lower bounds on central moments of cost accumulators.

Loop Invariant Analysis.

The main purpose of the current work is to enable automatic verification of inductive invariants for continuous probabilistic programs. Reasoning about probabilistic loops via quantitative invariants was first introduced by McIver and Morgan
(
McIver and Morgan, 2005
)
for probabilistic programs with discrete probabilistic choice and extended by various authors to different settings
(
Szymczak and Katoen, 2019
;
Gretz et al., 2014
;
Kaminski and Katoen, 2017
)
.
A generalization of this framework to programs featuring continuous distribution was proposed in
(
Chakarov and Sankaranarayanan, 2013
)
and uses super-martingale ranking functions as the probabilistic analogue of classic ranking functions.
The super-martingale approach has been specialized to qualitative and quantitative termination problems for various classes of programs such as polynomial programs
(
Chatterjee et al., 2016a
)
, affine programs admitting a linear ranking super-martingale
(
Chatterjee et al., 2016b
)
, and non-deterministic probabilistic programs
(
Chatterjee et al., 2017
;
Agrawal et al., 2018
)
.
Besides termination, this approach has been successfully applied also to cost analysis
(
Wang et al., 2019
)
and equivalence refutation
(
Chatterjee et al., 2024
)
.
While building on the martingale approach,
(
Chatterjee et al., 2022
)
overcomes the necessity to compute ranking martingales, by introducing the notion of stochastic invariants indicators, that can be used to prove termination in a sound and relatively complete way.
A different generalization was proposed in
(
Chakarov and Sankaranarayanan, 2014
)
, where expectation invariants are proposed to introduce a generalized fixed-point framework for programs with continuous distributions and unbounded loops.
However, this analysis rules out invariants expressed as Iverson brackets and is restricted to piecewise linear assertion guards and updates. Similarly,
(
Wang et al., 2021b
)
, restricts to affine programs and invariants to find exponential bounds on assertion violation probabilities.

Exact Inference.

Exact inference on probabilistic programs involving continuous distributions is especially hard because of the challenge of computing densities via integrals.
Exact symbolic inference engines like
(
Gehr et al., 2016
;
Gehr et al., 2020
;
Narayanan et al., 2016
;
Saad et al., 2021
)
only support deterministically bounded loops.
Even within this constrained context, the occurrence of non-simplified integrals in the output can significantly impede the potential to perform appropriate analysis or further computations.
A different, yet still exact approach is pursued in
(
Moosbrugger et al., 2022b
)
, where the focus is on the computation of moments as functions of the loop iteration.
In order to compute the moments exactly, the syntax of the programs is restricted quite severely, though parametric models are supported. Under the same syntactic restrictions, it has been shown in
(
Moosbrugger et al., 2022a
)
that termination probabilities can be computed automatically.

11.
Conclusion

We tackled the semi-automatic verification of user-provided quantitative invariants for probabilistic programs involving continuous distributions, conditioning, and potentially unbounded loops.
Our approach is to approximate the Lebesgue integrals appearing in a program’s weakest pre-expectation semantics by lower and upper Riemann sums.
This simple idea allows us to prove sound bounds on expected outcomes by verifying invariants automatically with SMT-solvers.
We demonstrated that this method can be readily integrated in the
Caesar
verification infrastructure
(
Schröer et al., 2023
)
.

There are many opportunities for future work.
A natural direction is to integrate our approach to invariant verification with techniques for
fully automatic invariant generation
such as
(
Batz et al., 2023
)
.
While we can already verify invariants for non-trivial benchmarks, there is some room for improvement on the engineering side.
This includes more sophisticated, multi-dimensional, and adaptive partitions of the integration domain, and a combination with direct integration methods, where applicable.
We also plan to extend the front end of
Caesar
with dedicated support for continuous distributions.

Due to its simplicity, we are confident that our approach is amenable to generalizations such as non-determinism and strategy synthesis as in
(
Batz et al., 2024
)
, and programs with pointers and data structures.

The authors thank Philipp Schroer for his support with
Caesar
, Mirco Tribastone for his help with the initial conceptualization of the paper, and the anonymous referees for their detailed comments.

https://www.dfg.de
Grant #GRK 2236 UnRAVeL, the EU’s Horizon 2020 programme under the Marie Skłodowska-Curie grant No. 101008233 (MISSION), the ERC AdG No. 787914 (FRAPPANT), and the PNRR project iNEST (Interconnected Nord-Est Innovation Ecosystem) funded by the European Union Next-GenerationEU (Piano Nazionale di Ripresa e Resilienza (PNRR) – Missione 4 Componente 2, Investimento 1.5 – D.D. 1058 23/06/2022, ECS_00000043).

Appendix A
Additional Preliminary Material

A.1.
Fixed Point Theory

The following observation is key in several proofs in this paper:

Lemma A.1 (
cf.
(
Abramsky and Jung, 1994
, Proposition 2.1.12)
).

Let
(L,\,\preceq)
be an
\omega
-cpo and let
a\colon\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}\to L
be monotonic (with regards to the usual order on
\mathbb{N}
lifted pointwise to
\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}
).
Then

\sup_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{j\in\mathbb{N}}a(i,j)~{}={}~\sup_{i\in\mathbb{N}}a(i,i)~{}={}~\sup_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{i\in\mathbb{N}}a(i,j)~.

Proof.

Section

C.1
.
∎

A.2.
Measure Theory and Lebesgue Integrals

A.2.1.
Basic Notions of Measure Theory

Most of the following can be found in
(
Ash and Doléans-Dade, 2000
)
.
Let
\Omega
be a set.
A
\sigma
-algebra on
\Omega
is a set
\mathcal{F}\subseteq 2^{\Omega}
of subsets of
\Omega
such that
\Omega\in\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{F}
is closed under taking countable unions and complements relative to
\Omega
.
If
\mathcal{F}
is a
\sigma
-algebra on
\Omega
then
(\Omega,\mathcal{F})
is called a
measurable space
and the sets in
\mathcal{F}
are called
measurable sets
(this is not to be confused with a
measure space
which is introduced further below).
For an arbitrary set
\mathcal{C}\subseteq 2^{\Omega}
of subsets of
\Omega
we let
\sigma(\mathcal{C})=\bigcap\{\mathcal{F}\mid\mathcal{C}\subseteq\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}\text{ a $\sigma$-algebra on }\Omega\}
be the smallest
\sigma
-algebra (on
\Omega
) containing
\mathcal{C}
.

The
Borel
\sigma
-algebra

{\mathcal{B}({\overline{\mathbb{R}}})}
on the extended real numbers
{\overline{\mathbb{R}}}
is the smallest
\sigma
-algebra containing all intervals of the form
(a,b]
,
a,b\in{\overline{\mathbb{R}}}
, i.e.,
{\mathcal{B}({\overline{\mathbb{R}}})}=\sigma(\{(a,b]\mid a,b\in{\overline{\mathbb{R}}}\})
.
The sets in
{\mathcal{B}({\overline{\mathbb{R}}})}
are called
Borel sets
.
Note that many common sets including various types of intervals (bounded, unbounded, open, closed, half-open, and so on), all countable subsets of
{\overline{\mathbb{R}}}
, and many others are Borel sets.
However,
{\mathcal{B}({\overline{\mathbb{R}}})}\neq 2^{\overline{\mathbb{R}}}
, i.e., there exist non-Borel sets.
For
A\in{\mathcal{B}({\overline{\mathbb{R}}})}
we define
{\mathcal{B}(A)}={\mathcal{B}({\overline{\mathbb{R}}})}\cap A
, where the intersection is taken element-wise.
It can be shown that
{\mathcal{B}(A)}
is a
\sigma
-algebra on
A
and
{\mathcal{B}(A)}\subseteq{\mathcal{B}({\overline{\mathbb{R}}})}
, see, e.g.,
(
Ash and Doléans-Dade, 2000
, page 5)
.
In this way we may obtain a
\sigma
-algebra on, say,
{{[0,1]}}
.

Given a measurable space
(\Omega,\mathcal{F})
, a
measure
on
\mathcal{F}
is a function
\bm{\mu}\colon\mathcal{F}\to{\overline{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}
such that for all countable collections
\mathcal{A}\subseteq\mathcal{F}
of pairwise disjoint subsets of
\mathcal{F}
(i.e.,
\forall A,B\in\mathcal{A}\colon A=B\lor A\cap B=\emptyset
) it holds that

\bm{\mu}\left(\bigcup_{A\in\mathcal{A}}A\right)=\sum_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\bm{\mu}(A)
, where the infinite sum is allowed to take value
\infty
.
If
\bm{\mu}(\Omega)=1
then
\bm{\mu}
is a
probability measure
.
We denote by
\bm{\lambda}
the
Lebesgue measure
19
19

19

We remark that it is also common to define
\bm{\lambda}
on
{\overline{\mathcal{B}}(\mathbb{R})}
, the
completion
of
{\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R})}
w.r.t.
\bm{\lambda}
(the sets in
{\overline{\mathcal{B}}(\mathbb{R})}
are often called
Lebesgue measurable
), but this construction is not necessary for our purposes.

on
{\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R})}

is the unique measure
\bm{\lambda}
satisfying
\bm{\lambda}((a,b])=b-a
for all
a\leq b\in\mathbb{R}
.
Note that we define this only on the reals as we do not need it for the extended reals.
In fact, the only measure we consider (explicitly) in this paper is the Lebesgue measure on
{\mathcal{B}({{[0,1]}})}
.

A.2.2.
Lebesgue Integrals

Let
(\Omega_{i},\mathcal{F}_{i})
,
i\in\{1,2\}
, be measurable spaces.
A function
f\colon\Omega_{1}\to\Omega_{2}
is called
measurable
w.r.t.
\mathcal{F}_{1}
and
\mathcal{F}_{2}
if for all
A\in\mathcal{F}_{2}
it holds that
f^{-1}(A)\in\mathcal{F}_{1}
.

For our purpose we only need to define integrals of non-negative functions.
Let
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\bm{\mu})
be a measure space and
f\colon\Omega\to{\overline{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}
be an arbitrary function.
A measurable function
s\colon\Omega\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
w.r.t.
\mathcal{F}
and
{\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0})}
is called
simple
if its image
s(\Omega)
is a finite set
\{a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}\}
; such an
s
can be written as
s(x)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}\cdot{\left[x\in s^{-1}(a_{i})\right]}
for all
x\in\Omega
.
The Lebesgue integral of the simple function
s
on
A\in\mathcal{F}
is defined as

\int_{A}s\,d\bm{\mu}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}\cdot\bm{\mu}(s^{-1}(a_{i})\cap A)

.
The Lebesgue integral of
f
on
A
is then defined as

\int_{A}f\,d\bm{\mu}~{}={}~\sup\left\{\int_{A}s\,d\bm{\mu}\mid s\text{ is simple, }s\leq f\text{ (pointwise)}\right\}

which can be any number in
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
or
\infty
.
Note that
f
itself does not have to be measurable; however, many fundamental properties of Lebesgue integrals break for non-measurable
f
, hence it is customary to consider Lebesgue integrals only for measurable
f
.

A.2.3.
Multi- vs. Single-dimensional Integrals

Given two measurable spaces
(\Omega_{i},\mathcal{F}_{i})
,
i\in\{1,2\}
, define
\mathcal{F}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{2}=\sigma(\{A_{1}\times A_{2}\mid A_{i}\in\mathcal{F}_{i}\})
.
The measurable space
(\Omega_{1}\times\Omega_{2},\mathcal{F}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{2})
is called the
product
of
(\Omega_{1},\mathcal{F}_{1})
and
(\Omega_{2},\mathcal{F}_{2})
.
For
n>1
and
A\in{\mathcal{B}({\overline{\mathbb{R}}})}
, we obtain the Borel
\sigma
-algebra
{\mathcal{B}(A^{n})}
on
A^{n}
as the
n
-fold product of
(A,{\mathcal{B}(A)})
with itself.
The sets in
{\mathcal{B}(A^{n})}
are called Borel sets as well.

We call a function
f\colon A\to B

Borel measurable
if both
A
and
B
are Borel sets (in any dimension) and
f
is measurable w.r.t. the respective Borel
\sigma
-algebras on
A
and
B
.
In this paper, we often consider Borel measurable functions of type
f\colon\mathbb{R}^{n}\to{\overline{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}
and we would like to do something like
“take a Lebesgue integral in one variable”
.
To justify that this makes sense (and preserves measurability) we rely on the following:

Theorem A.2 (Part of Fubini’s Theorem

(
Rudin, 1987
, Theorems 8.5 and 8.8)
).

Let
(\Omega_{i},\mathcal{F}_{i})
,
i\in\{1,2\}
, be measurable spaces and
f\colon\Omega_{1}\times\Omega_{2}\to{\overline{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}
be measurable w.r.t. the product space.
Then:

(1)

For every
x\in\Omega_{1}
, the function
f_{x}\colon\Omega_{2}\to{\overline{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0},y\mapsto f(x,y)
is measurable w.r.t.
\mathcal{F}_{2}
.

(2)

For every
A\in\mathcal{F}_{2}
and measure
\bm{\mu}
on
\mathcal{F}_{2}
, the function
F\colon\Omega_{1}\to{\overline{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0},x\mapsto\int_{A}f_{x}\,d\bm{\mu}
is measurable w.r.t.
\mathcal{F}_{1}
.

For a given
x\in\Omega_{1}
, we also write
\int_{A}f(x,y)\,d\bm{\mu}(y)
instead of
\int_{A}f_{x}\,d\bm{\mu}
.

A.3.
Riemann Integrals

A useful aspect of (lower and upper) Riemann integrals is that one can choose partitions that induce sequences of upper (resp. lower) sums that converge
monotonically
.
For partitions
P=(x_{0},\ldots,x_{N})
and
Q=(y_{0},\ldots,y_{M})
,
both of the same interval, we say that
P

refines

Q
, in symbols
P\preceq Q
, if for all indices
0\leq j\leq M
there exists
0\leq i\leq N
such that
x_{i}=y_{j}
.
In other words, the partition
P
can be obtained from
Q
by subdividing some of the intervals in
Q
further.

Lemma A.3 (
see, e.g.,
(
Fitzpatrick, 2009
, Lemma 6.2)
).

Let
f\colon{[a,b]}\to\mathbb{R}
be bounded.
Suppose that
P\preceq Q
.
Then
{L_{f,Q}}\leq{L_{f,P}}

and

{U_{f,P}}\leq{U_{f,Q}}
.

To obtain the Riemann-Darboux integral, it is not actually necessary to consider
all
partitions.
Indeed, it suffices to consider a set of partitions whose
norm
(
“fineness”
) becomes arbitrarily small.
We now formalize this.
For a partition
P=(x_{0},\ldots,x_{N})\in\mathfrak{P}{[a,b]}
, let its norm be defined as

\|P\|~{}={}~\max_{0\leq i<N}x_{i+1}-x_{i}~.

Theorem A.4 (
see, e.g.,
(
Fitzpatrick, 2009
, Theorem 7.12)
).

Let
f\colon{[a,b]}\to\mathbb{R}
be bounded and let
(P_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence of partitions of
{[a,b]}
satisfying
\lim_{n\to\infty}\|P_{n}\|=0
.
Then

\underline{\int_{a}^{b}}f(x)\,dx~{}={}~\lim_{n\to\infty}{L_{f,P_{n}}}~{}={}~\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}{L_{f,P_{n}}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\overline{\int_{a}^{b}}f(x)\,dx~{}={}~\lim_{n\to\infty}{U_{f,P_{n}}}~{}={}~\inf_{n\in\mathbb{N}}{U_{f,P_{n}}}~.

Proof.

Section

C.2

∎

Appendix B
Omitted Remarks and Explanations

B.1.
On Redundancies in the Program Syntax

Some of the programming constructs from
Definition

4.1
could be regarded syntactic sugar.
However, this would have some unsatisfactory effects.
For example,
\{C_{1}\}~[p]~\{C_{2}\}
is equivalent to
\mathtt{tmp}:\approx\mathtt{unif}_{{{[0,1]}}}\,;\,\mathtt{if}(\mathtt{tmp}<p)~\{C_{1}\}~\mathtt{else}~\{C_{2}\}
, but this has the disadvantages that (i) it requires an additional program variable, (ii) it assumes that
(\mathtt{tmp}<p)\in\mathcal{G}
, and (iii) it replaces a simple convex combination by a way more difficult integration operation, which is a major drawback in practice.
Further, one could define
\mathtt{diverge}
as syntactic sugar for
\mathtt{while}~(true)~\{\mathtt{skip}\}
.
However, in the context of this paper, it is more convenient to distinguish between loopy programs and loop-free programs possibly containing
\mathtt{diverge}
.

B.2.
Formal Definition of Loop Unrolling

The following definition appears in
Section

7.2
.
Let
0pt\in\mathbb{N}
.
We define the
0pt
-fold unrolling

C^{0pt}
of program
C
inductively as follows:
For all atomic programs
C
, we let
C^{0pt}=C
.
Further, we set (using some parentheses – which are of course not part of the program syntax – for clarity)

•

(C_{1}\,;\,C_{2})^{0pt}=C_{1}^{0pt}\,;\,C_{2}^{0pt}
,

•

(\mathtt{if}(\varphi)~\{C_{1}\}~\mathtt{else}~\{C_{2}\})^{0pt}=\mathtt{if}(\varphi)~\{C_{1}^{0pt}\}~\mathtt{else}~\{C_{2}^{0pt}\}
, and

•

(\{C_{1}\}~[p]~\{C_{2}\})^{0pt}=\{C_{1}^{0pt}\}~[p]~\{C_{2}^{0pt}\}
.

For loops, we let

•

(\mathtt{while}~(\varphi)~\{B\})^{0}=\mathtt{diverge}
, and for
0pt\geq 0
,

•

(\mathtt{while}~(\varphi)~\{B\})^{0pt+1}=\mathtt{if}(\varphi)~\{B^{0pt}\,;\,(\mathtt{while}~(\varphi)~\{B\})^{0pt}\}~\mathtt{else}~\{\mathtt{skip}\}
.

Appendix C
Proofs

C.1.
Proof of
Lemma

A.1

See
A.1

Proof.

The suprema and infima exist because
(L,\,\preceq)
is an
\omega
-cpo.
The fact that the order of the two suprema can be exchanged is a standard property of suprema, so we only show the leftmost equality:
For all
i\in\mathbb{N}
,
\sup_{j}a(i,j)\geq a(i,i)
, so
\sup_{i}\sup_{j}a(i,j)\geq\sup_{i}a(i,i)
.
For the other inequality we argue as follows:
Let
i\in\mathbb{N}
be arbitrary.

\displaystyle\sup_{j}a(i,j)

(because
a
is non-decreasing in the 2nd argument)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\sup_{i\leq j}a(i,j)

(because
i\leq j
and
a
is non-decreasing in the 1st argument)

\displaystyle~{}\leq{}~

\displaystyle\sup_{i\leq j}a(j,j)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\sup_{j}a(j,j)

Since
i
was arbitrary it follows that
\sup_{i}\sup_{j}a(i,j)\leq\sup_{j}a(j,j)
.
∎

C.2.
Proof of
Theorem

A.4

See
A.4

Proof.

We only show the equalities for the lower integral.
The statement for the upper integral follows with
Lemma

C.1
.

We first show that
\underline{\int_{a}^{b}}f(x)\,dx=\lim_{n\to\infty}{L_{f,P_{n}}}
.
Let us fix
\varepsilon>0
.
We want to prove that there exists
n_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\forall n\geq n_{0}
it holds that
\underline{\int_{a}^{b}}f-{L_{f,P_{n}}}<\varepsilon
.

By definition of
\underline{\int_{a}^{b}}f
as the supremum over all partitions there exists a partition
P_{\varepsilon}
such that

\underline{\int_{a}^{b}}f-{L_{f,P_{\varepsilon}}}<\frac{\varepsilon}{2}.

Let
N
be the number of points in
P_{\varepsilon}
and suppose that
f
is bounded by
B
, i.e.,
|f|\leq B
.
Since
\lim_{n\to\infty}\|P_{n}\|=0
there exists
n_{0}
such that
\forall n\geq n_{0}
we have
\|P_{n}\|<\frac{\varepsilon}{4NB}
.
Let
n\geq n_{0}
be fixed for the rest of the proof.

We set
P_{n}^{*}=P_{n}\cup P_{\varepsilon}
, i.e., the partition obtained considering as extrema of the sub-intervals all the points in
P_{n}
and
P_{\varepsilon}
.
Then
P_{n}^{*}\preceq P_{n}
and
P_{n}^{*}\preceq P_{\varepsilon}
,
and thus
{L_{f,P_{n}^{*}}}\geq{L_{f,P_{n}}}
and
{L_{f,P_{n}^{*}}}\geq{L_{f,P_{\varepsilon}}}
.
Let us consider the difference
{L_{f,P_{n}^{*}}}-{L_{f,P_{n}}}
.
Let
I
be the set of indexes
i
such that
x_{i}\in P_{n}
and
[x_{i},x_{i+1}]
contains at least one point
x_{i}^{\varepsilon}
of
P_{\varepsilon}
.
Then
|I|\leq N
and

\displaystyle{L_{f,P_{n}^{*}}}-{L_{f,P_{n}}}

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in I}\left((x_{i+1}-x_{i}^{\varepsilon})\inf_{[x_{i}^{\varepsilon},x_{i+1}]}f(x)+(x_{i}^{\varepsilon}-x_{i})\inf_{[x_{i},x_{i}^{\varepsilon}]}f(x)~{}-{}~(x_{i+1}-x_{i})\inf_{[x_{i},x_{i+1}]}f(x)\right)

\displaystyle\begin{split}~{}={}~&\sum_{i\in I}\left((x_{i+1}-x_{i}^{\varepsilon})\inf_{[x_{i}^{\varepsilon},x_{i+1}]}f(x)+(x_{i}^{\varepsilon}-x_{i})\inf_{[x_{i},x_{i}^{\varepsilon}]}f(x)\right.\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.~{}-{}~(x_{i+1}-x_{i}^{\varepsilon})\inf_{[x_{i},x_{i+1}]}f(x)-(x_{i}^{\varepsilon}-x_{i})\inf_{[x_{i},x_{i+1}]}f(x)\right)\end{split}

\displaystyle~{}\leq{}~

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in I}(x_{i+1}-x_{i}^{\varepsilon})\underbrace{\left(\inf_{[x_{i}^{\varepsilon},x_{i+1}]}f(x)-\inf_{[x_{i},x_{i+1}]}f(x)\right)}_{\leq 2B}~{}+{}~\sum_{i\in I}(x_{i}^{\varepsilon}-x_{i})\underbrace{\left(\inf_{[x_{i},x_{i}^{\varepsilon}]}f(x)-\inf_{[x_{i},x_{i+1}]}f(x)\right)}_{\leq 2B}

\displaystyle~{}\leq{}~

\displaystyle N\frac{\varepsilon}{4NB}2B~{}={}~\frac{\varepsilon}{2}~.

It follows that

\underline{\int_{a}^{b}}f-{L_{f,P_{n}}}~{}={}~\underline{\int_{a}^{b}}f-{L_{f,P_{n}^{*}}}+{L_{f,P_{n}^{*}}}-{L_{f,P_{n}}}~{}\leq{}~\underline{\int_{a}^{b}}f-{L_{f,P_{\varepsilon}}}+L_{f,P_{n}^{*}}-{L_{f,P_{n}}}<\frac{\varepsilon}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}=\varepsilon~.

It remains to show that
\lim_{n\to\infty}{L_{f,P_{n}}}=\sup_{n\geq 0}{L_{f,P_{n}}}
.
By definition of the lower integral as the supremum over the lower sums w.r.t.
every possible
partition it follows that
\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}{L_{f,P_{n}}}\leq\underline{\int_{a}^{b}}f
.
On the other hand, the limit of a convergent sequence is always bounded by its supremum, i.e.,
\lim_{n\to\infty}{L_{f,P_{n}}}\leq\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}{L_{f,P_{n}}}

∎

Lemma C.1.

For bounded
f\colon{[a,b]}\to\mathbb{R}
and
P\in\mathfrak{P}{[a,b]}
it holds that

(1)

{L_{-f,P}}=-{U_{f,P}}
, and

(2)

\underline{\int_{a}^{b}}-f(x)\,dx=-\overline{\int_{a}^{b}}f(x)\,dx
.

Proof.

Let
P=(x_{0},\ldots,x_{N})
.
For (1) note that

\displaystyle{L_{-f,P}}~{}={}~

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}(x_{i+1}-x_{i})\inf_{x\in{[x_{i},x_{i+1}]}}-f(x)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}(x_{i+1}-x_{i})\cdot\left(-\sup_{x\in{[x_{i},x_{i+1}]}}f(x)\right)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle-\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}(x_{i+1}-x_{i})\sup_{x\in{[x_{i},x_{i+1}]}}f(x)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle-{U_{f,P}}~.

For (2) we argue similarly:

\displaystyle\underline{\int_{a}^{b}}-f(x)\,dx~{}={}~

\displaystyle\sup\left\{{L_{-f,P}}\mid P\in\mathfrak{P}{[a,b]}\right\}

(by (1))

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\sup\left\{-{U_{f,P}}\mid P\in\mathfrak{P}{[a,b]}\right\}

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle-\inf\left\{{U_{f,P}}\mid P\in\mathfrak{P}{[a,b]}\right\}

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle-\overline{\int_{a}^{b}}f(x)\,dx~.

∎

C.3.
Proof of
Lemma

4.3

See
4.3

Proof.

To see that
(\mathbb{E},\,\sqsubseteq)
and
({\mathbb{E}^{\leq 1}},\,\sqsubseteq)
are complete lattices for any
A\subseteq\mathbb{E}
(resp.
A\subseteq{\mathbb{E}^{\leq 1}}
) set

\sup A=\lambda\sigma.\sup_{f\in A}f(\sigma),\hskip 28.45274pt\inf A=\lambda\sigma.\inf_{f\in A}f(\sigma).

Then,
\sup A\in\mathbb{E}
and
\inf A\in\mathbb{E}
(resp.
\sup A\in{\mathbb{E}^{\leq 1}}
and
\inf A\in{\mathbb{E}^{\leq 1}}
).

Theorem 11.17 from
(
Rudin, 1953
)
ensures that for any sequence of measurable functions the supremum and infimum of the sequence is measurable. Specializing the theorem in the case of Borel measurability ensures that
({\mathbb{E}_{meas}},\,\sqsubseteq)
and
({\mathbb{E}^{\leq 1}_{meas}},\,\sqsubseteq)
are
\omega
-bicpos.
∎

C.4.
Proof of
Theorem

4.5

See
4.5

Proof.

Parts of this statement have been shown in in
(
Szymczak and Katoen, 2019
, p. 88 (Proof of Lemma 3))
and
(
Gretz et al., 2014
, Lemma 34)
. Here we provide a mostly self-contained proof.
Note that for well-definedness, one must show various points:
preservation of measurable expectations, existence of the Lebesgue integrals, and existence of the least and greatest fixed points – for the latter,
\omega
-(co)continuity is needed, which is why we show it simultaneously.

In the following, we let
f\in{\mathbb{E}_{meas}}
be an arbitrary measurable expectation and
f_{0}\sqsubseteq f_{1}\sqsubseteq\ldots
a (non-decreasing)
\omega
-chain in
{\mathbb{E}_{meas}}
.
Similarly,
g\in{\mathbb{E}^{\leq 1}_{meas}}
is a 1-bounded measurable expectation and
g_{0}\sqsupseteq g_{1}\sqsupseteq\ldots
a (non-increasing)
\omega
-cochain in
{\mathbb{E}^{\leq 1}_{meas}}
.
We will show by structural induction that for all
C\in\mathsf{pWhile}
it holds that
\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(f\right)
and
\mathsf{wlp}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(g\right)
are measurable and that
\sup_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(f_{i}\right)=\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\sup_{i\in\mathbb{N}}f_{i}\right)
as well as
\inf_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\mathsf{wlp}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(g_{i}\right)=\mathsf{wlp}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\inf_{i\in\mathbb{N}}g_{i}\right)
.
Throughout this proof we write
\sup_{i}
as a shorthand for
\sup_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
.

The case
C=\mathtt{skip}

\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\mathtt{skip}\rrbracket=\mathsf{wlp}\llbracket\mathtt{skip}\rrbracket
is the identify function, hence it trivially preserves measurable expectations and is
\omega
-bicontinuous.

The case
C=\mathtt{diverge}

\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\mathtt{diverge}\rrbracket
are
\mathsf{wlp}\llbracket\mathtt{diverge}\rrbracket
are constant expectations, hence they are both measurable and
\omega
-bicontinuous.

The case
C=\mathtt{x}\coloneq E

By definition,
\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\mathtt{x}\coloneq E\rrbracket\left(f\right)=f[\mathtt{x}/E]
which can also be written as
f\circ U
where
U\colon\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{V}\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{V},\sigma\mapsto\sigma[\mathtt{x}\mapsto E(\sigma)]
is a function updating the program state according to
\mathtt{x}\coloneq E
.
Since
E
is measurable,
U
is measurable as well, and thus
f[\mathtt{x}/E]
is measurable as a composition of measurable functions. The argument for measurability of
\mathsf{wlp}\llbracket\mathtt{x}\coloneq E\rrbracket\left(g\right)
is exactly the same.

For
\omega
-continuity we have:

\displaystyle\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\mathtt{x}\coloneq E\rrbracket\left(\sup_{i}f_{i}\right)

(definition of
\mathsf{wp}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\left(\sup_{i}f_{i}\right)[\mathtt{x}/E]

(
\sup
defined pointwise)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\sup_{i}f_{i}[\mathtt{x}/E]

(definition of
\mathsf{wp}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\sup_{i}\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\mathtt{x}\coloneq E\rrbracket\left(f_{i}\right)~.

An analogous argument holds for
\omega
-cocontinuity of
\mathsf{wlp}\llbracket\mathtt{x}\coloneq E\rrbracket
.

The case
C=\mathtt{x}:\approx\mathtt{unif}_{{{[0,1]}}}

Measurability of
\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\mathtt{x}:\approx\mathtt{unif}_{{{[0,1]}}}\rrbracket\left(f\right)=\lambda\sigma.\int_{{[0,1]}}f(\sigma[\mathtt{x}\mapsto\xi])\,d\bm{\lambda}(\xi)
and its liberal counterpart is a direct application of
Theorem

A.2

For
\omega
-continuity of
\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\mathtt{x}:\approx\mathtt{unif}_{{{[0,1]}}}\rrbracket
we argue as follows:

\displaystyle\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\mathtt{x}:\approx\mathtt{unif}_{{{[0,1]}}}\rrbracket\left(\sup_{i}f_{i}\right)

(definition of
\mathsf{wp}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\lambda\sigma.\int_{{[0,1]}}\left(\sup_{i}f_{i}\right)(\sigma[\mathtt{x}\mapsto\xi])\,d\bm{\lambda}(\xi)

(
\sup
defined pointwise)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\lambda\sigma.\int_{{[0,1]}}\sup_{i}f_{i}(\sigma[\mathtt{x}\mapsto\xi])\,d\bm{\lambda}(\xi)

(Monotone Convergence Theorem, e.g.,
(
Schilling, 2017
, Thm. 12.1)
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\lambda\sigma.\sup_{i}\int_{{[0,1]}}f_{i}(\sigma[\mathtt{x}\mapsto\xi])\,d\bm{\lambda}(\xi)

(
\sup
defined pointwise)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\sup_{i}\lambda\sigma.\int_{{[0,1]}}f_{i}(\sigma[\mathtt{x}\mapsto\xi])\,d\bm{\lambda}(\xi)

(definition of
\mathsf{wp}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\sup_{i}\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\mathtt{x}:\approx\mathtt{unif}_{{{[0,1]}}}\rrbracket\left(f_{i}\right)

Note the application of the the Monotone Convergence Theorem, which ensures that for a non-decreasing sequence of measurable
{\overline{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}
-valued functions it is possible to switch the supremum over the sequence and the Lebesgue integral. Co-continuity for
\mathsf{wlp}
follows by expressing
\inf_{i}g_{i}=1-\sup_{i}(1-g_{i})
and observing that the functions
(1-g_{i})\in{\mathbb{E}^{\leq 1}}
are a non-decreasing sequence, so Monotone Converge Theorem can be applied to them.

The case
C=\mathtt{observe}(\varphi)

Since we require the functions in
\mathcal{G}
to be Borel measurable,
{\left[\varphi\right]}
is Borel measurable for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{G}
. Measurability of
\mathsf{wp}
and
\mathsf{wlp}
then follows from the fact that pointwise product of measurable functions is measurable.
For
\omega
-continuity we have:

\displaystyle\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\mathtt{observe}(\varphi)\rrbracket\left(\sup_{i}f_{i}\right)

(definition of
\mathsf{wp}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\sup_{i}f_{i}

(
\sup
defined pointwise)

\displaystyle~{}={}~\sup_{i}\left({\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot f_{i}\right)

(Definition of
\mathsf{wp}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~\sup_{i}\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\mathtt{observe}(\varphi)\rrbracket\left(f_{i}\right)

where, again, we are using the properties of the supremum taken with respect to pointwise ordering as in
(
Szymczak and Katoen, 2019
)
. The same holds for
\omega
-cocontinuity of
\mathsf{wlp}
using the properties of the infimum taken with respect to pointwise ordering.

The case
C=\mathtt{if}(\varphi)~\{C_{1}\}~\mathtt{else}~\{C_{2}\}

By I.H. we know that
\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(f\right)
and
\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)
are measurable and
\omega
-continuous. Then, measurability of
\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\mathtt{if}(\varphi)~\{C_{1}\}~\mathtt{else}~\{C_{2}\}\rrbracket\left(f\right)={\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(f\right)+{\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)
follows from the fact that pointwise sum and product of measurable functions is measurable.
For
\omega
-continuity we have:

\displaystyle\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\mathtt{if}(\varphi)~\{C_{1}\}~\mathtt{else}~\{C_{2}\}\rrbracket\left(\sup_{i}f_{i}\right)

(definition of
\mathsf{wp}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(\sup_{i}f_{i}\right)+{\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(\sup_{i}f_{i}\right)

(I.H.)

\displaystyle~{}={}~{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\sup_{i}\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(f_{i}\right)+{\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot\sup_{i}\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f_{i}\right)

(pointwise ordering)

\displaystyle~{}={}~\sup_{i}{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(f_{i}\right)+\sup_{i}{\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f_{i}\right)

(properties of
\sup
)

\displaystyle~{}\geq{}~\sup_{i}{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(f_{i}\right)+{\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f_{i}\right)

To prove that inverse inequality holds, we proceed by contradiction, supposing
>
holds. Then, for some
i_{1},i_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
it holds:

{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(f_{i_{1}}\right)+{\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f_{i_{2}}\right)>\sup_{i}{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(f_{i}\right)+{\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f_{i}\right).

However, since
f_{i}
is a
\omega
-chain we can take
k\in\mathbb{N}
such that
k>i_{i}
and
k>i_{2}
for which we have

\displaystyle{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(f_{k}\right)+{\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f_{k}\right)

(monotonicity of
\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket,\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket
)

\displaystyle\geq{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(f_{i_{1}}\right)+{\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f_{i_{2}}\right)

(hypothesis)

\displaystyle>\sup_{i}{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(f_{i}\right)+{\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f_{i}\right)

which is a contradiction since

{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(f_{k}\right)+{\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f_{k}\right)\leq\sup_{i}{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(f_{i}\right)+{\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f_{i}\right).

For
\mathsf{wlp}
both measurability and
\omega
-cocontinuity can be proved using the same arguments.

The case
C=\{C_{1}\}~[p]~\{C_{2}\}

As in the previous case, replacing
{\left[\varphi\right]}
with
p
and
{\left[\neg\varphi\right]}
with
1-p
.

The case
C=C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}

Measurability of
\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)
and
\mathsf{wlp}\llbracket C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rrbracket\left(g\right)
follows immediately from the I.H..
For
\omega
-continuity we have:

\displaystyle\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rrbracket\left(\sup_{i}f_{i}\right)

(definition of
\mathsf{wp}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(\sup_{i}f_{i}\right)\right)

(I.H. on
\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(\sup_{i}\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f_{i}\right)\right)

(I.H. on
\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~\sup_{i}\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f_{i}\right)\right)

(definition of
\mathsf{wp}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~\sup_{i}\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f_{i}\right)

where
\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(\sup_{i}\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f_{i}\right)\right)
is well-defined since by I.H.
\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f_{i}\right)
is measurable for every
i
and therefore
\sup_{i}\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f_{i}\right)
is measurable as well.

The case
C=\mathtt{while}~(\varphi)~\{B\}

\displaystyle\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\sup_{i}f_{i}\right)

(definition of
\mathsf{wp}
)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{lfp}\,{\lambda g.{\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot\sup_{i}f_{i}+{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket B\rrbracket(g)}=

(I.H. continuity of
\mathsf{wp}\llbracket B\rrbracket
)

\displaystyle=\sup_{n}\left({\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot\sup_{i}f_{i}+{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket B\rrbracket\right)^{n}(0)=

(continuity of
\lambda f.{\left[\neg\varphi\right]}f+{\left[\varphi\right]}\mathsf{wp}\llbracket B\rrbracket\left(g\right)
)

\displaystyle=\sup_{n}\left(\sup_{i}{\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot f_{i}+{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket B\rrbracket\right)^{n}(0)=

(
*
)

\displaystyle=\sup_{n}\sup_{i}\left({\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot f_{i}+{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket B\rrbracket\right)^{n}(0)=

(
**
)

\displaystyle=\sup_{i}\sup_{n}({\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot f_{i}+{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket B\rrbracket)^{n}(0)=

(I.H. continuity of
\mathsf{wp}\llbracket B\rrbracket
)

\displaystyle=\sup_{i}\mathsf{lfp}\,{\lambda g.{\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot f_{i}+{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket B\rrbracket(g)}=

(definition of
\mathsf{wp}
)

\displaystyle=\sup_{i}\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(f_{i}\right).

where
(*)
is justified by proving with an inner induction on
n
that:

\left(\sup_{i}{\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot f_{i}+{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C\rrbracket\right)^{n}(0)=\sup_{i}\left({\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot f_{i}+{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C\rrbracket\right)^{n}(0).

In fact, for
n=1
the equality holds trivially, and assuming it holds for
n-1
we get:

L.H.S.

\displaystyle=\left(\sup_{i}{\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot f_{i}+{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C\rrbracket\right)\left(\sup_{j}{\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot f_{j}+{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C\rrbracket\right)^{n-1}(0)=

(case
n-1
)

\displaystyle=\left(\sup_{i}{\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot f_{i}+{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C\rrbracket\right)\left(\sup_{j}\left({\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot f_{j}+{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C\rrbracket\right)^{n-1}(0)\right)=

(outer I.H.)

\displaystyle=\sup_{j}\left(\sup_{i}{\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot f_{i}+{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C\rrbracket\right)\left(\left({\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot f_{j}+{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C\rrbracket\right)^{n-1}(0)\right)=

(case
n=1
)

\displaystyle=\sup_{j}\sup_{i}\left({\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot f_{i}+{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C\rrbracket\right)\left(\left({\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot f_{j}+{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C\rrbracket\right)^{n-1}(0)\right)=

(Lemma
A.1
)

\displaystyle=\sup_{i}\left({\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot f_{i}+{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C\rrbracket\right)\left(\left({\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot f_{i}+{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C\rrbracket\right)^{n-1}(0)\right)=

\displaystyle=\sup_{i}\left(\left({\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot f_{i}+{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C\rrbracket\right)^{n}(0)\right)=\text{R.H.S.}.

For
(**)
switching the order of suprema is justified by the fact that both terms exists.

To prove co-continuity of
\mathsf{wlp}
we can use the same arguments:
(*)
and
(**)
still hold if
\sup
is replaced by
\inf
, since Lemma
A.1
still holds and the order of infima can be switched if both terms of the equality exist.
∎

C.5.
Proof of
Lemma

5.4

See
5.4

Proof.

We will show that the statement holds for
\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}\llbracket C\rrbracket
as the proof in the remaining cases is analogous. We proceed by structural induction on
C
. For
C\in\{\mathtt{skip},\mathtt{diverge},\mathtt{x}\coloneq E,\mathtt{observe}(\varphi)\}

\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}\llbracket C\rrbracket
is equal to
\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C\rrbracket
so the conclusion holds by monotonicity of the latter
(
McIver and Morgan, 2005
)
.

For
C=\mathtt{x}:\approx\mathtt{unif}_{{{[0,1]}}}
suppose
f\sqsubseteq g
, then:

\displaystyle\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(f\right)

(definition of
\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\inf_{\xi\in[\frac{i}{N},\frac{i+1}{N}]}f[\mathtt{x}/\xi]

(
f\sqsubseteq g
)

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\inf_{\xi\in[\frac{i}{N},\frac{i+1}{N}]}g[\mathtt{x}/\xi]

(definition of
\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(f\right)

For
C\in\{\mathtt{if}(\varphi)~\{C_{1}\}~\mathtt{else}~\{C_{2}\},\{p\}~[C_{1}]~\{C_{2}\},C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\}
follows by I.H. on
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
.

For
C=\mathtt{while}~(\varphi)~\{B\}
consider the characteristic functions
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-14.51323pt{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.67207pt{C}\kern 5.70836pt{{f}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-14.51323pt{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.67207pt{C}\kern 5.70836pt{{f}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-12.34377pt{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}\kern 3.5389pt}}_{{\kern-6.40187pt{C}\kern 3.5389pt{{f}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-11.33264pt{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}\kern 2.52777pt}}_{{\kern-5.39075pt{C}\kern 2.52777pt{{f}}}}}
and
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-14.51323pt{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.67207pt{C}\kern 5.70836pt{{g}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-14.51323pt{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.67207pt{C}\kern 5.70836pt{{g}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-12.34377pt{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}\kern 3.5389pt}}_{{\kern-6.40187pt{C}\kern 3.5389pt{{g}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-11.33264pt{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}\kern 2.52777pt}}_{{\kern-5.39075pt{C}\kern 2.52777pt{{g}}}}}
.
Let us denote by
Y_{f}
and
Y_{g}
the least fixed points of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-14.51323pt{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.67207pt{C}\kern 5.70836pt{{f}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-14.51323pt{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.67207pt{C}\kern 5.70836pt{{f}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-12.34377pt{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}\kern 3.5389pt}}_{{\kern-6.40187pt{C}\kern 3.5389pt{{f}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-11.33264pt{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}\kern 2.52777pt}}_{{\kern-5.39075pt{C}\kern 2.52777pt{{f}}}}}
and
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-14.51323pt{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.67207pt{C}\kern 5.70836pt{{g}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-14.51323pt{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.67207pt{C}\kern 5.70836pt{{g}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-12.34377pt{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}\kern 3.5389pt}}_{{\kern-6.40187pt{C}\kern 3.5389pt{{g}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-11.33264pt{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}\kern 2.52777pt}}_{{\kern-5.39075pt{C}\kern 2.52777pt{{g}}}}}
respectively. From I.H. it follows that
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-14.51323pt{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.67207pt{C}\kern 5.70836pt{{f}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-14.51323pt{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.67207pt{C}\kern 5.70836pt{{f}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-12.34377pt{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}\kern 3.5389pt}}_{{\kern-6.40187pt{C}\kern 3.5389pt{{f}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-11.33264pt{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}\kern 2.52777pt}}_{{\kern-5.39075pt{C}\kern 2.52777pt{{f}}}}}(Y)\sqsubseteq\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-14.51323pt{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.67207pt{C}\kern 5.70836pt{{g}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-14.51323pt{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.67207pt{C}\kern 5.70836pt{{g}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-12.34377pt{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}\kern 3.5389pt}}_{{\kern-6.40187pt{C}\kern 3.5389pt{{g}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-11.33264pt{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}\kern 2.52777pt}}_{{\kern-5.39075pt{C}\kern 2.52777pt{{g}}}}}(Y)
for all
Y
. Then,
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-14.51323pt{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.67207pt{C}\kern 5.70836pt{{f}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-14.51323pt{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.67207pt{C}\kern 5.70836pt{{f}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-12.34377pt{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}\kern 3.5389pt}}_{{\kern-6.40187pt{C}\kern 3.5389pt{{f}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-11.33264pt{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}\kern 2.52777pt}}_{{\kern-5.39075pt{C}\kern 2.52777pt{{f}}}}}(Y_{g})\sqsubseteq\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-14.51323pt{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.67207pt{C}\kern 5.70836pt{{g}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-14.51323pt{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.67207pt{C}\kern 5.70836pt{{g}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-12.34377pt{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}\kern 3.5389pt}}_{{\kern-6.40187pt{C}\kern 3.5389pt{{g}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-11.33264pt{\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}}\kern 2.52777pt}}_{{\kern-5.39075pt{C}\kern 2.52777pt{{g}}}}}(Y_{g})=Y_{g}.
By Theorem
3.2
the previous implies
Y_{f}\sqsubseteq Y_{g}
.
∎

C.6.
Counter-example to
\omega
-continuity of
\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}

Consider
C=\mathtt{x}:\approx\mathtt{unif}_{{{[0,1]}}}
and the sequence of functions
f_{i}=1-\sum_{k=i}^{\infty}{\left[\mathtt{x}=\frac{1}{k}\right]}
. Then
\sup_{i}f_{i}=1
and therefore
\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\sup_{i}f_{i}\right)=1.

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\sup_{i}\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}\llbracket C\rrbracket(f_{i})

(definition of
\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\sup_{i}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\inf_{\xi\in\left[\frac{j}{N},\frac{j+1}{N}\right]}f_{i}[\mathtt{x}/\xi]

(splitting the summation)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\sup_{i}\left(\frac{1}{N}\inf_{\xi\in\left[\frac{0}{N},\frac{1}{N}\right]}f_{i}[\mathtt{x}/\xi]+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\inf_{\xi\in\left[\frac{j}{N},\frac{j+1}{N}\right]}f_{i}[\mathtt{x}/\xi]\right)

(
\inf_{\xi\in\left[\frac{0}{N},\frac{1}{N}\right]}f_{i}[\mathtt{x}/\xi]=0\,\forall i
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{i}\left(\frac{N-1}{N}\right)

(sup of a constant sequence)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\frac{N-1}{N}

C.7.
Proof of
Lemma

5.5

See
5.5

Proof.

We prove the statement for
\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}\llbracket C\rrbracket
since the remaining cases are analogous.

We proceed by structural induction on
C
.

For
C\in\{\mathtt{skip},\mathtt{diverge},\mathtt{x}\coloneq E,\mathtt{observe}(\varphi)\}
the definitions of
\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}\llbracket C\rrbracket
and
\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C\rrbracket
coincide so pointwise inequality holds trivially.

For
C=\mathtt{x}:\approx\mathtt{unif}_{{{[0,1]}}}
we have

\displaystyle\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(f\right)

(definition of
\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\inf_{\xi\in\left[\frac{i}{N},\frac{i+1}{N}\right]}f[\mathtt{x}/\xi]

(integral of constant function)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\int_{\frac{i}{N}}^{\frac{i+1}{N}}\inf_{\xi\in\left[\frac{i}{N},\frac{i+1}{N}\right]}f[\mathtt{x}/\xi]\,d\bm{\lambda}(\xi)

(monotonicity of integral)

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\int_{\frac{i}{N}}^{\frac{i+1}{N}}f[\mathtt{x}/\xi]\,d\bm{\lambda}{\xi}

(elementary property of integral)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}f[\mathtt{x}/\xi]\,d\bm{\lambda}(\xi)

(definition of
\mathsf{wp}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(f\right).

For
C\in\{\mathtt{if}(\varphi)~\{C_{1}\}~\mathtt{else}~\{C_{2}\},\{p\}~[C_{1}]~\{C_{2}\}\}
conclusion follows applying the I.H. on
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
.

For
C=C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}
we have

\displaystyle\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(f\right)=

(definition of
\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\right)

(I.H. on
C_{2}
+ Lemma
5.4
)

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\,

\displaystyle\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\right)

(I.H. on
C_{1}
)

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\,

\displaystyle\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\right)

(definition of
\mathsf{wp}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(f\right).

Finally, for
C=\mathtt{while}~(\varphi)~\{B\}
consider the characteristic functions
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathsf{wp}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-11.69948pt{\mathsf{wp}}\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.67207pt{C}\kern 5.70836pt{{f}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathsf{wp}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-11.69948pt{\mathsf{wp}}\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.67207pt{C}\kern 5.70836pt{{f}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathsf{wp}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-7.85pt{\mathsf{wp}}\kern 3.5389pt}}_{{\kern-6.40187pt{C}\kern 3.5389pt{{f}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathsf{wp}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-6.83888pt{\mathsf{wp}}\kern 2.52777pt}}_{{\kern-5.39075pt{C}\kern 2.52777pt{{f}}}}}
and
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-17.04489pt{\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}}\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.67207pt{C}\kern 5.70836pt{{f}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-17.04489pt{\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}}\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.67207pt{C}\kern 5.70836pt{{f}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-14.1521pt{\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}}\kern 3.5389pt}}_{{\kern-6.40187pt{C}\kern 3.5389pt{{f}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-13.14098pt{\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}}\kern 2.52777pt}}_{{\kern-5.39075pt{C}\kern 2.52777pt{{f}}}}}
.
Let
Y^{*}
and
Y^{*}_{N}
be the least fixed points of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathsf{wp}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-11.69948pt{\mathsf{wp}}\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.67207pt{C}\kern 5.70836pt{{f}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathsf{wp}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-11.69948pt{\mathsf{wp}}\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.67207pt{C}\kern 5.70836pt{{f}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathsf{wp}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-7.85pt{\mathsf{wp}}\kern 3.5389pt}}_{{\kern-6.40187pt{C}\kern 3.5389pt{{f}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathsf{wp}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-6.83888pt{\mathsf{wp}}\kern 2.52777pt}}_{{\kern-5.39075pt{C}\kern 2.52777pt{{f}}}}}
and
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-17.04489pt{\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}}\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.67207pt{C}\kern 5.70836pt{{f}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-17.04489pt{\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}}\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.67207pt{C}\kern 5.70836pt{{f}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-14.1521pt{\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}}\kern 3.5389pt}}_{{\kern-6.40187pt{C}\kern 3.5389pt{{f}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-13.14098pt{\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}}\kern 2.52777pt}}_{{\kern-5.39075pt{C}\kern 2.52777pt{{f}}}}}
respectively.
From I.H. on
B
it follows
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-17.04489pt{\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}}\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.67207pt{C}\kern 5.70836pt{{f}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-17.04489pt{\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}}\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.67207pt{C}\kern 5.70836pt{{f}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-14.1521pt{\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}}\kern 3.5389pt}}_{{\kern-6.40187pt{C}\kern 3.5389pt{{f}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-13.14098pt{\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}}\kern 2.52777pt}}_{{\kern-5.39075pt{C}\kern 2.52777pt{{f}}}}}(Y)\sqsubseteq\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathsf{wp}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-11.69948pt{\mathsf{wp}}\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.67207pt{C}\kern 5.70836pt{{f}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathsf{wp}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-11.69948pt{\mathsf{wp}}\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.67207pt{C}\kern 5.70836pt{{f}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathsf{wp}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-7.85pt{\mathsf{wp}}\kern 3.5389pt}}_{{\kern-6.40187pt{C}\kern 3.5389pt{{f}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathsf{wp}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-6.83888pt{\mathsf{wp}}\kern 2.52777pt}}_{{\kern-5.39075pt{C}\kern 2.52777pt{{f}}}}}(Y)
for all
Y
.
Then,
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-17.04489pt{\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}}\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.67207pt{C}\kern 5.70836pt{{f}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-17.04489pt{\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}}\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.67207pt{C}\kern 5.70836pt{{f}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-14.1521pt{\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}}\kern 3.5389pt}}_{{\kern-6.40187pt{C}\kern 3.5389pt{{f}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-13.14098pt{\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}}\kern 2.52777pt}}_{{\kern-5.39075pt{C}\kern 2.52777pt{{f}}}}}(Y^{*})\sqsubseteq\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathsf{wp}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-11.69948pt{\mathsf{wp}}\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.67207pt{C}\kern 5.70836pt{{f}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathsf{wp}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-11.69948pt{\mathsf{wp}}\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.67207pt{C}\kern 5.70836pt{{f}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathsf{wp}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-7.85pt{\mathsf{wp}}\kern 3.5389pt}}_{{\kern-6.40187pt{C}\kern 3.5389pt{{f}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathsf{wp}}}}_{{{C}}}}\Phi^{{\kern-6.83888pt{\mathsf{wp}}\kern 2.52777pt}}_{{\kern-5.39075pt{C}\kern 2.52777pt{{f}}}}}(Y^{*})=Y^{*}
and from Theorem
3.2
it follows
Y^{*}_{N}\sqsubseteq Y^{*}
.
∎

C.8.
Proof of
Lemma

C.2

Lemma C.2.

For all programs
C\in\mathsf{pWhile}
and post-expectations
f\in\mathbb{E}
,
\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{N}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(f\right)
and
\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{2^{N}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(f\right)
are non-increasing (non-decreasing, respectively) sequences in
N\geq 1
.

Proof.

Let
N\geq 1
be fixed.
We show by induction on the structure of
C
that for all
f\in\mathbb{E}
it holds that
\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{N}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(f\right)\leq\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{N+1}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(f\right)
.
The base case
C=\mathtt{x}:\approx\mathtt{unif}_{{{[0,1]}}}
follows with
Lemma

A.3
because the partition
0<\frac{1}{2^{N}}<\frac{2}{2^{N}}\ldots<1
is refined by
0<\frac{1}{2^{N+1}}<\frac{2}{2^{N+1}}=\frac{1}{2^{N}}<\ldots<1
.
For the other base cases there is nothing to show.
The inductive cases are straightforward using monotonicity of
\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{N}}\llbracket C\rrbracket
.
We exemplify this for sequential composition:

\displaystyle\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{N}}\llbracket C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)

(definition of
\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{N}}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{N}}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{N}}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\right)

(I.H. on
C_{2}
and monotonicity of
\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{N}}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket
(
Lemma

5.4
))

\displaystyle~{}\sqsubseteq{}~

\displaystyle\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{N}}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{N+1}}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\right)

(I.H. on
C_{1}
)

\displaystyle~{}\sqsubseteq{}~

\displaystyle\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{N+1}}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{N+1}}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\right)

(definition of
\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{N+1}}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{N+1}}\llbracket C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)~.

The argument for
\overline{\mathsf{wp}}
is similar.
∎

C.9.
Proof of
Lemma

C.3

Let
{\mathbb{E}_{meas}^{\lesssim}}
be the set of measurable locally bounded expectations.

Lemma C.3.

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a set of locally bounded measurable arithmetic expressions (
“right hand sides”
) and
\mathcal{G}
be an arbitrary set of guards.
Let
C\in\mathsf{pWhile}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{G})
be loop-free.
Then
\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C\rrbracket
preserves
{\mathbb{E}_{meas}^{\lesssim}}
.
Moreover,
\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C\rrbracket
preserves limits of non-increasing sequences from
{\mathbb{E}_{meas}^{\lesssim}}
.

Proof.

By induction on the structure of
C
.
Let
f\in{\mathbb{E}_{meas}^{\lesssim}}
be locally bounded and let
f_{0}\sqsupseteq f_{1}\sqsupseteq\ldots
be a non-increasing sequence of expectations from
{\mathbb{E}_{meas}^{\lesssim}}
.

The case
C=\mathtt{skip}

Trivial.

The case
C=\mathtt{diverge}

Trivial.

The case
C=\mathtt{observe}(\varphi)

Straightforward.

The case
C=\mathtt{x}\coloneq E

We have to show that
\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\mathtt{x}\coloneq E\rrbracket\left(f\right)=f[\mathtt{x}/E]=\lambda\sigma.f(\sigma[\mathtt{x}\mapsto E(\sigma)])
is locally bounded, i.e.,
that

\sup_{\sigma\in K}f(\sigma[\mathtt{x}\mapsto E(\sigma)])<\infty

for all compact
K\subseteq\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{V}
.

Let us fix a compact
K\subseteq\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{V}
.
Since
E
is locally bounded by assumption, we have
E(K)\subseteq[0,b]
for some
b\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
.
Now let
K^{\prime}\supseteq K
be a compact set such that for all
\sigma\in K
and
\xi\in[0,b]
we have
\sigma[\mathtt{x}\mapsto\xi]\in K^{\prime}
. To see that
K^{\prime}
exists consider the projection of
K
on
\mathbb{R}^{V\setminus\{\mathtt{x}\}}
, denoted by
\pi(K)
. By Theorem 39 in
(
Pugh and Pugh, 2002
)

\pi(K)
is compact. Then set
K^{\prime}=\pi(K)\times[0,b]
. By Theorem 31 in
(
Pugh and Pugh, 2002
)

K^{\prime}
is compact and
\sigma[\mathtt{x}\mapsto\xi]\in K^{\prime}
for all
\sigma\in K
and
\xi\in[0,b]
.

Then we have

\{f(\sigma[\mathtt{x}\mapsto E(\sigma)])\mid\sigma\in K\}~\subseteq~\{f(\sigma[\mathtt{x}\mapsto\xi])\mid\sigma\in K,\xi\in[0,b]\}~\subseteq~\{f(\sigma)\mid\sigma\in K^{\prime}\}

and thus
\sup_{\sigma\in K}f(\sigma[\mathtt{x}\mapsto E(\sigma)])\leq\sup_{\sigma\in K^{\prime}}f(\sigma)<\infty
because
f
is locally bounded.

The case
C=\mathtt{x}:\approx\mathtt{unif}_{{{[0,1]}}}

We first show that

\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\mathtt{x}:\approx\mathtt{unif}_{{{[0,1]}}}\rrbracket\left(f\right)=\lambda\sigma.\int_{{[0,1]}}f(\sigma[\mathtt{x}\mapsto\xi])\,d\bm{\lambda}(\xi)

remains locally bounded.
To this end let
K\subseteq\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{V}
be compact.
Recall that we have to show that
\sup\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\mathtt{x}:\approx\mathtt{unif}_{{{[0,1]}}}\rrbracket\left(f\right)(K)<\infty
.
Let
K^{\prime}\supseteq K
be a compact set such that for all
\sigma\in K
and
\xi\in{{[0,1]}}
we have
\sigma[\mathtt{x}\mapsto\xi]\in K^{\prime}
, where
K^{\prime}
can be constructed as above.
Since
f
is locally bounded we have
\sup f(K^{\prime})=b\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
.
Hence for all
\sigma\in K^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\int_{{[0,1]}}f(\sigma[\mathtt{x}\mapsto\xi])\,d\bm{\lambda}(\xi)

\displaystyle~{}\leq{}~

\displaystyle\int_{{[0,1]}}\sup f(K^{\prime})\,d\bm{\lambda}(\xi)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\int_{{[0,1]}}b\,d\bm{\lambda}(\xi)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle b

It follows that
\sup\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\mathtt{x}:\approx\mathtt{unif}_{{{[0,1]}}}\rrbracket\left(f\right)(K)\leq\sup\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\mathtt{x}:\approx\mathtt{unif}_{{{[0,1]}}}\rrbracket\left(f\right)(K^{\prime})\leq b<\infty
and hence the claim.

The fact that
\mathsf{wp}
preserve infima of non-increasing sequences follows from the properties of pointwise
\inf
.

The fact that
\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\mathtt{x}:\approx\mathtt{unif}_{{{[0,1]}}}\rrbracket
preserves infima of non-increasing sequences
(f_{i})
follows from an extended version of the Monotone Convergence
(
Schilling, 2017
, Theorem 12.1 (ii))
adapted to non-increasing sequences of functions. Notably, the corollary requires that the integral of the first function in the sequence is smaller than
\infty
, but this is guaranteed by the restriction to locally bounded expectations, since for every
\sigma\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{V}
we can consider the compact set
K_{\sigma}=\{\sigma[\mathtt{x}\mapsto\xi]\mid\xi\in{{[0,1]}}\}
, and since
f_{0}
is locally bounded, we find that
\int_{{[0,1]}}f_{0}(\sigma[\mathtt{x}\mapsto\xi])\,d\bm{\lambda}(\xi)\leq\sup K_{\sigma}<\infty
.

The case
C=\mathtt{if}(\varphi)~\{C_{1}\}~\mathtt{else}~\{C_{2}\}

Local boundedness follows by applying the I.H. to
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
, while preservation of the limit can be proved using the same argument as in the proof
Theorem

4.5
.

The case
C=\{C_{1}\}~[p]~\{C_{2}\}

Local boundedness follows by applying the I.H. to
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
, while preservation of the limit can be proved using the same argument as in the proof
Theorem

4.5
.

The case
C=C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}

Since
\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)=\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\right)
it follows immediately from the I.H. that
\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\right)\in{\mathbb{E}_{meas}^{\lesssim}}
.

To conclude the proof observe that

\displaystyle\inf_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f_{i}\right)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\inf_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f_{i}\right)\right)

(I.H. on
C_{1}
, using that
\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f_{i}\right)
is a non-decreasing sequence in
{\mathbb{E}_{meas}^{\lesssim}}
by I.H. on
C_{2}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(\inf_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f_{i}\right)\right)

(I.H. on
C_{2}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(\inf_{i\in\mathbb{N}}f_{i}\right)\right)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rrbracket\left(\inf_{i\in\mathbb{N}}f_{i}\right)

∎

C.10.
Proof of
Lemma

7.4

See
7.4

Proof.

In the rest of the proof we write
\sup_{N}
and
\inf_{N}
instead of
\sup_{N\geq 1}
and
\inf_{N\geq 1}
, respectively.
We will show the following
alternative
statement:
For all loop-free
C\in\mathsf{pWhile}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{G})
and post-expectations
f\in\mathcal{F}
:
(
2
) from above holds, and

(
3
’)

\displaystyle\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(f\right)~{}={}~\sup_{N}\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{N}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(f\right)~{}={}~\inf_{N}\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{2^{N}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(f\right)~.

Note that the difference between (
’
‣
C.10
) and
(
3
)

is that we consider the supremum over powers of two.
This simplifies the situation as
\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{N}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(f\right)
and
\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{2^{N}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(f\right)
are non-decreasing (non-increasing, respectively) sequences in
N
(see
Lemma

C.2
), which allows us to apply
Lemma

A.1
at a crucial point in the proof.

To show that the alternative statement implies the claim of
Lemma

7.4
we argue as follows:
By properties of suprema,
\sup_{N}\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{N}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(f\right)\leq\sup_{N}\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(f\right)
.
We also have

\displaystyle\sup_{N}\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(f\right)

(by
Lemma

5.5
)

\displaystyle~{}\leq{}~

\displaystyle\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(f\right)

(by the alternative statement (
’
‣
C.10
))

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\sup_{N}\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{N}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(f\right)~,

and thus
\sup_{N}\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{N}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(f\right)=\sup_{N}\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(f\right)
.
The argument for
\inf_{N}\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{2^{N}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(f\right)=\inf_{N}\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(f\right)
is analogous.

The proof of the alternative statement (
’
‣
C.10
) is by induction over the structure of
C
.
In the following, we let
f\in\mathcal{F}
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
be arbitrary.

We consider the base cases first.
For the base cases
C\in\{\mathtt{skip},\mathtt{diverge},\mathtt{x}\coloneq E,\mathtt{observe}(\varphi)\}
the definitions of
\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}
,
\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}
, and
\mathsf{wp}
coincide, so we only have to show that
\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(f\right)\in\mathcal{F}
.

The case
C=\mathtt{skip}

By definition,
\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\mathtt{skip}\rrbracket\left(f\right)=f\in\mathcal{F}
.

The case
C=\mathtt{diverge}

By definition,
\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\mathtt{diverge}\rrbracket\left(f\right)=0
.
By assumption,
0\in\mathcal{F}
.

The case
C=\mathtt{x}\coloneq E

By definition,
\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\mathtt{x}\coloneq E\rrbracket\left(f\right)=f[\mathtt{x}/E]
.
By assumption,
f[\mathtt{x}/E]\in\mathcal{F}
.

The case
C=\mathtt{observe}(\varphi)

By definition,
\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\mathtt{observe}(\varphi)\rrbracket\left(f\right)={\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot f
.
By assumption,
{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot f+{\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot 0\in\mathcal{F}
.

The case
C=\mathtt{x}:\approx\mathtt{unif}_{{{[0,1]}}}

This base case is more interesting and crucially relies on Riemann-integrability.
We first show that
\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}\llbracket\mathtt{x}:\approx\mathtt{unif}_{{{[0,1]}}}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\in\mathcal{F}
.
By definition of
\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}
,

\displaystyle\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}\llbracket\mathtt{x}:\approx\mathtt{unif}_{{{[0,1]}}}\rrbracket\left(f\right)~{}={}~\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\inf_{\xi\in[\frac{i}{N},\frac{i+1}{N}]}f[\mathtt{x}/\xi]~.

The right-hand side is a finite convex combination of suprema over subintervals of
{{[0,1]}}
, hence it is in
\mathcal{F}
by assumption.
Next we show

\sup_{N}\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{N}}\llbracket\mathtt{x}:\approx\mathtt{unif}_{{{[0,1]}}}\rrbracket\left(f\right)=\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\mathtt{x}:\approx\mathtt{unif}_{{{[0,1]}}}\rrbracket\left(f\right)
:

\displaystyle\sup_{N}\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{N}}\llbracket\mathtt{x}:\approx\mathtt{unif}_{{{[0,1]}}}\rrbracket\left(f\right)

(definition of
\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{N}}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\sup_{N}\frac{1}{2^{N}}\sum_{i=0}^{2^{N}-1}\inf_{\xi\in[\frac{i}{2^{N}},\frac{i+1}{2^{N}}]}f[\mathtt{x}/\xi]

(by
Theorem

A.4
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\lambda\sigma.\underline{\int_{0}^{1}}f(\sigma[\mathtt{x}\mapsto\xi])\,d\xi

(by assumption
f\in\mathcal{F}
is bounded and Riemann-integrable w.r.t.
\mathtt{x}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\lambda\sigma.\int_{0}^{1}f(\sigma[\mathtt{x}\mapsto\xi])\,d\xi

(Riemann integral = Lebesgue integral, see
Theorem

7.2
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\lambda\sigma.\int_{{[0,1]}}f(\sigma[\mathtt{x}\mapsto\xi])\,d\bm{\lambda}(\xi)

(definition of
\mathsf{wp}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\mathtt{x}:\approx\mathtt{unif}_{{{[0,1]}}}\rrbracket\left(f\right)

The proof for
\overline{\mathsf{wp}}
is exactly analogous (replace all infima by suprema and vice versa).

The case
C=\mathtt{if}(\varphi)~\{C_{1}\}~\mathtt{else}~\{C_{2}\}

\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}\llbracket\mathtt{if}(\varphi)~\{C_{1}\}~\mathtt{else}~\{C_{2}\}\rrbracket\left(f\right)=[\varphi]\cdot\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(f\right)+[\neg\varphi]\cdot\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\in\mathcal{F}
by I.H. on
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
and assumption.
Furthermore,

\displaystyle\sup_{N}\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{N}}\llbracket\mathtt{if}(\varphi)~\{C_{1}\}~\mathtt{else}~\{C_{2}\}\rrbracket\left(f\right)

(definition of
\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\sup_{N}\left({\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{N}}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(f\right)+{\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{N}}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\right)

(addition and multiplication by Iverson brackets is
\omega
-continuous)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\sup_{N}\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{N}}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(f\right)+{\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot\sup_{N}\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{N}}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)

(by I.H. on
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(f\right)+{\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)

(definition of
\mathsf{wp}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\mathtt{if}(\varphi)~\{C_{1}\}~\mathtt{else}~\{C_{2}\}\rrbracket\left(f\right)~.

Again, the proof for
\overline{\mathsf{wp}}
is analogous (consider infima instead of suprema, and use that addition and multiplication by Iverson brackets is
\omega
-
co
continuous).

The case
C=\{C_{1}\}~[p]~\{C_{2}\}

\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}\llbracket\{C_{1}\}~[p]~\{C_{2}\}\rrbracket\left(f\right)=p\cdot\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(f\right)+(1-p)\cdot\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\in\mathcal{F}
by I.H. on
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
and assumption.
Furthermore,

\displaystyle\sup_{N}\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{N}}\llbracket\{C_{1}\}~[p]~\{C_{2}\}\rrbracket\left(f\right)

(definition of
\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\sup_{N}\left(p\cdot\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{N}}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(f\right)+(1-p)\cdot\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{N}}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\right)

(addition and multiplication by constants is
\omega
-continuous)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle p\cdot\sup_{N}\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{N}}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(f\right)+(1-p)\cdot\sup_{N}\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{N}}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)

(by I.H. on
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle p\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(f\right)+(1-p)\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)

(definition of
\mathsf{wp}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\{C_{1}\}~[p]~\{C_{2}\}\rrbracket\left(f\right)~.

As before, the proof for
\overline{\mathsf{wp}}
is analogous (consider infima instead of suprema, and use that addition and multiplication by constants is
\omega
-
co
continuous).

The case
C=C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}

First, note that
\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}\llbracket C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)=\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\right)\in\mathcal{F}
by I.H. on
C_{2}
and
C_{1}
.
For convergence we argue as follows:

\displaystyle\sup_{N}\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{N}}\llbracket C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)

(definition of
\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\sup_{N}\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{N}}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{N}}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\right)

(
Lemma

A.1
and
Lemma

C.2
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\sup_{M}\sup_{N}\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{N}}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{M}}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\right)

(I.H. on
C_{1}
, using that
\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{M}}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\in\mathcal{F}
(also by I.H.))

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\sup_{M}\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{M}}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\right)

(
\omega
-continuity of
\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket
, see
Theorem

4.5
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(\sup_{M}\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{M}}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\right)

(I.H. on
C_{2}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\right){f}

(definition of
\mathsf{wp}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)

The convergence proof of
\overline{\mathsf{wp}}
is not fully analogous as
\mathsf{wp}
is not
\omega
-
co
continuous in general.
Instead, we invoke
Lemma

C.3
:

\displaystyle\inf_{N}\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{2^{N}}\llbracket C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)

(definition of
\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\inf_{N}\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{2^{N}}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{2^{N}}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\right)

(
“
\inf
-version”
of
Lemma

A.1
and
Lemma

C.2
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\inf_{M}\inf_{N}\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{2^{N}}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{2^{M}}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\right)

(I.H. on
C_{1}
, using that
\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{2^{M}}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\in\mathcal{F}
(also by I.H.))

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\inf_{M}\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{2^{M}}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\right)

(
Lemma

C.3
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(\inf_{M}\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{2^{M}}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\right)

(I.H. on
C_{2}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\right){f}

(definition of
\mathsf{wp}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)

∎

C.11.
\mathsf{wlp}
-Version of
Lemma

7.4

Lemma C.4.

Let
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F})
be Riemann-suitable.
Then for all loop-free
C\in\mathsf{pWhile}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{G})
and post-expectations
g\in\mathcal{F}\cap{\mathbb{E}^{\leq 1}_{meas}}
the following holds:

\displaystyle\forall N\geq 1\colon\quad\underline{\mathsf{wl}}\mathsf{p}^{N}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(g\right)\in\mathcal{F}\quad\text{and}\quad\overline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{l}\overline{\mathsf{p}}^{N}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(g\right)\in\mathcal{F}

and

\displaystyle\mathsf{wlp}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(g\right)~{}={}~\sup_{N\geq 1}\underline{\mathsf{wl}}\mathsf{p}^{N}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(g\right)~{}={}~\inf_{N\geq 1}\overline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{l}\overline{\mathsf{p}}^{N}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(g\right)~.

Proof.

Very similar to the proof of
Lemma

7.4
.
A minor difference is the straightforward base case
C=\mathtt{diverge}
.
Also recall that
\mathsf{wlp}
only applies to 1-bounded expectations only.
∎

C.12.
Proof of
Lemma

7.5

See
7.5

Proof.

The proof is by induction on the structure of
C
.
We focus on
\mathsf{wp}
, the proof for
\mathsf{wlp}
is analogous.
For the bases cases
C=\mathtt{skip}
,
C=\mathtt{diverge}
,
C=\mathtt{x}\coloneq E
,
C=\mathtt{x}:\approx\mathtt{unif}_{{{[0,1]}}}
and
C=\mathtt{observe}(\varphi)
there is nothing to show as these atomic programs are unaffected by unfolding.
We now consider the composite cases.
Let
f\in{\mathbb{E}_{meas}}
be arbitrary but fixed throughout the following.

The case
C=\mathtt{if}(\varphi)~\{C_{1}\}~\mathtt{else}~\{C_{2}\}
and
C=\{C_{1}\}~[p]~\{C_{2}\}

These cases follow straightforwardly by induction and basic properties of
\mathsf{wp}
.

The case
C=C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}

To show that for all
0pt\in\mathbb{N}
it holds that
\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C^{0pt}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\sqsubseteq\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C^{0pt+1}\rrbracket\left(f\right)
we argue as follows:

\displaystyle\mathsf{wp}\llbracket(C_{1}\,;\,C_{2})^{0}pt\rrbracket\left(f\right)

(definition of
\mathsf{wp}
and of
(C_{1}\,;\,C_{2})^{0}pt
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}^{0}pt\rrbracket\left(\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{2}^{0}pt\rrbracket\left(f\right)\right)

(I.H. on
C_{2}
, monotonicity of
\mathsf{wp}
)

\displaystyle~{}\sqsubseteq{}~

\displaystyle\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}^{0}pt\rrbracket\left(\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{2}^{0pt+1}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\right)

(I.H. on
C_{1}
)

\displaystyle~{}\sqsubseteq{}~

\displaystyle\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}^{0pt+1}\rrbracket\left(\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{2}^{0pt+1}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\right)

(definition of
\mathsf{wp}
and of
(C_{1}\,;\,C_{2})^{0pt+1}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\mathsf{wp}\llbracket(C_{1}\,;\,C_{2})^{0pt+1}\rrbracket\left(f\right)

To see that the supremum over all unrollings is equal to the exact
\mathsf{wp}
consider the following:

\displaystyle\sup_{0pt\in\mathbb{N}}\mathsf{wp}\llbracket(C_{1}\,;\,C_{2})^{0}pt\rrbracket\left(f\right)

(definition of
\mathsf{wp}
and of
(C_{1}\,;\,C_{2})^{0}pt
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\sup_{0pt\in\mathbb{N}}\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}^{0}pt\rrbracket\left(\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{2}^{0}pt\rrbracket\left(f\right)\right)

(
Lemma

A.1
, monotonicity of
\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}^{0pt_{1}}\rrbracket
, I.H. on
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\sup_{0pt_{1}\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{0pt_{2}\in\mathbb{N}}\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}^{0pt_{1}}\rrbracket\left(\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{2}^{0pt_{2}}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\right)

(
\omega
-continuity of
