\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}^{0pt_{1}}\rrbracket
, see
Theorem

4.5
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\sup_{0pt_{1}\in\mathbb{N}}\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}^{0pt_{1}}\rrbracket\left(\sup_{0pt_{2}\in\mathbb{N}}\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{2}^{0pt_{2}}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\right)

(I.H. on
C_{2}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\sup_{0pt_{1}\in\mathbb{N}}\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}^{0pt_{1}}\rrbracket\left(\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\right)

(I.H. on
C_{1}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\right)

(definition of
\mathsf{wp}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{1}\,;\,C_{2}\rrbracket\left(f\right)

The case
C=\mathtt{while}~(\varphi)~\{B\}

We first show by (an inner) induction on
0pt\in\mathbb{N}
that

\mathsf{wp}\llbracket(\mathtt{while}~(\varphi)~\{B\})^{0pt}\rrbracket\left(f\right)~{}\sqsubseteq{}~\mathsf{wp}\llbracket(\mathtt{while}~(\varphi)~\{B\})^{0pt+1}\rrbracket\left(f\right)~.

For
0pt=0
this follows as
\mathsf{wp}\llbracket(\mathtt{while}~(\varphi)~\{B\})^{0}\rrbracket\left(f\right)=\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\mathtt{diverge}\rrbracket\left(f\right)=0
by definition.
For
0pt>0
:

\displaystyle\mathsf{wp}\llbracket(\mathtt{while}~(\varphi)~\{B\})^{0pt}\rrbracket\left(f\right)

(definition of unfolding)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\mathtt{if}(\varphi)~\{B^{0pt-1}\,;\,(\mathtt{while}~(\varphi)~\{B\})^{0pt-1}\}~\mathtt{else}~\{\mathtt{skip}\}\rrbracket\left(f\right)

(definition of
\mathsf{wp}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket B^{0pt-1}\rrbracket\left(\mathsf{wp}\llbracket(\mathtt{while}~(\varphi)~\{B\})^{0pt-1}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\right)+{\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\mathtt{skip}\rrbracket\left(f\right)

(inner I.H., monotonicity of
\mathsf{wp}
)

\displaystyle~{}\sqsubseteq{}~

\displaystyle{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket B^{0pt-1}\rrbracket\left(\mathsf{wp}\llbracket(\mathtt{while}~(\varphi)~\{B\})^{0pt}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\right)+{\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\mathtt{skip}\rrbracket\left(f\right)

(outer I.H. on
B
)

\displaystyle~{}\sqsubseteq{}~

\displaystyle{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket B^{0pt}\rrbracket\left(\mathsf{wp}\llbracket(\mathtt{while}~(\varphi)~\{B\})^{0pt}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\right)+{\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\mathtt{skip}\rrbracket\left(f\right)

(definition of
\mathsf{wp}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\mathtt{if}(\varphi)~\{B^{0pt}\,;\,(\mathtt{while}~(\varphi)~\{B\})^{0pt}\}~\mathtt{else}~\{\mathtt{skip}\}\rrbracket\left(f\right)

(definition of unfolding)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\mathsf{wp}\llbracket(\mathtt{while}~(\varphi)~\{B\})^{0pt+1}\rrbracket\left(f\right)

To see that the supremum over all unrollings is equal to the exact
\mathsf{wp}
consider the following:

\displaystyle\sup_{0pt\in\mathbb{N}}\mathsf{wp}\llbracket(\mathtt{while}~(\varphi)~\{B\})^{0pt}\rrbracket\left(f\right)

(non-decreasing, see above)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\sup_{0pt\in\mathbb{N}}\mathsf{wp}\llbracket(\mathtt{while}~(\varphi)~\{B\})^{0pt+1}\rrbracket\left(f\right)

(definition of unfolding)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\sup_{0pt\in\mathbb{N}}\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\mathtt{if}(\varphi)~\{B^{0pt}\,;\,(\mathtt{while}~(\varphi)~\{B\})^{0pt}\}~\mathtt{else}~\{\mathtt{skip}\}\rrbracket\left(f\right)

(definition of
\mathsf{wp}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\sup_{0pt\in\mathbb{N}}\left({\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket B^{0pt}\rrbracket\left(\mathsf{wp}\llbracket(\mathtt{while}~(\varphi)~\{B\})^{0pt}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\right)+{\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot f\right)

(
\omega
-continuity of
\cdot
and
+
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\sup_{0pt\in\mathbb{N}}\mathsf{wp}\llbracket B^{0pt}\rrbracket\left(\mathsf{wp}\llbracket(\mathtt{while}~(\varphi)~\{B\})^{0pt}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\right)+{\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot f

(
Lemma

A.1
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\sup_{0pt_{2}\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{0pt_{1}\in\mathbb{N}}\mathsf{wp}\llbracket B^{0pt_{1}}\rrbracket\left(\mathsf{wp}\llbracket(\mathtt{while}~(\varphi)~\{B\})^{0pt_{2}}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\right)+{\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot f

(I.H. on
B
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\sup_{0pt_{2}\in\mathbb{N}}\mathsf{wp}\llbracket B\rrbracket\left(\mathsf{wp}\llbracket(\mathtt{while}~(\varphi)~\{B\})^{0pt_{2}}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\right)+{\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot f

(
\omega
-continuity of
\mathsf{wp}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket B\rrbracket\left(\sup_{0pt_{2}\in\mathbb{N}}\mathsf{wp}\llbracket(\mathtt{while}~(\varphi)~\{B\})^{0pt_{2}}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\right)+{\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot f

By the definition of
\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\mathtt{while}~(\varphi)~\{B\}\rrbracket
in terms of a least fixed point, the above equation implies

\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\mathtt{while}~(\varphi)~\{B\}\rrbracket\left(f\right)~{}\sqsubseteq{}~\sup_{0pt\in\mathbb{N}}\mathsf{wp}\llbracket(\mathtt{while}~(\varphi)~\{B\})^{0pt}\rrbracket\left(f\right)~.

To complete the proof we show by (an inner) induction on
0pt\in\mathbb{N}
that

\mathsf{wp}\llbracket(\mathtt{while}~(\varphi)~\{B\})^{0pt}\rrbracket\left(f\right)~{}\sqsubseteq{}~\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\mathtt{while}~(\varphi)~\{B\}\rrbracket\left(f\right)~.

This implies that
\sup_{0pt\in\mathbb{N}}\mathsf{wp}\llbracket(\mathtt{while}~(\varphi)~\{B\})^{0pt}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\sqsubseteq\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\mathtt{while}~(\varphi)~\{B\}\rrbracket\left(f\right)
.
For
0pt=0
this holds as
\mathsf{wp}\llbracket(\mathtt{while}~(\varphi)~\{B\})^{0}\rrbracket\left(f\right)=\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\mathtt{diverge}\rrbracket\left(f\right)=0
by definition.
For
0pt\geq 0
the argument is as follows:

\displaystyle\mathsf{wp}\llbracket(\mathtt{while}~(\varphi)~\{B\})^{0pt+1}\rrbracket\left(f\right)

(definition of unfolding)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\mathtt{if}(\varphi)~\{B^{0pt}\,;\,(\mathtt{while}~(\varphi)~\{B\})^{0pt}\}~\mathtt{else}~\{\mathtt{skip}\}\rrbracket\left(f\right)

(definition of
\mathsf{wp}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket B^{0pt}\rrbracket\left(\mathsf{wp}\llbracket(\mathtt{while}~(\varphi)~\{B\})^{0pt}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\right)+{\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot f

(inner I.H., monotonicity of
\mathsf{wp}\llbracket B^{0pt}\rrbracket
)

\displaystyle~{}\sqsubseteq{}~

\displaystyle{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket B^{0pt}\rrbracket\left(\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\mathtt{while}~(\varphi)~\{B\}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\right)+{\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot f

(outer I.H. on
B
)

\displaystyle~{}\sqsubseteq{}~

\displaystyle{\left[\varphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{wp}\llbracket B\rrbracket\left(\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\mathtt{while}~(\varphi)~\{B\}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\right)+{\left[\neg\varphi\right]}\cdot f

(definition of
\mathsf{wp}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\mathsf{wp}\llbracket\mathtt{while}~(\varphi)~\{B\}\rrbracket\left(f\right)

∎

C.13.
Proof of
Theorem

7.6

See
7.6

Proof.

We only show the equality involving
\mathsf{wp}
(the one for
\mathsf{wlp}
is analogous).
For all
n\in\mathbb{N}
we have

\displaystyle\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{n}\llbracket C^{n}\rrbracket\left(f\right)

(by
Lemma

5.5
)

\displaystyle~{}\leq{}~

\displaystyle\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C^{n}\rrbracket\left(f\right)

(by
Lemma

7.5
)

\displaystyle~{}\leq{}~

\displaystyle\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(f\right)

and thus
\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{n}\llbracket C^{n}\rrbracket\left(f\right)\leq\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(f\right)
.
But we also have

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{n}\llbracket C^{n}\rrbracket\left(f\right)

(elementary property of supremum)

\displaystyle~{}\geq{}~

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{n}}\llbracket C^{2^{n}}\rrbracket\left(f\right)

(by
Lemma

A.1
and
Lemma

C.2
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\sup_{0pt\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{2^{N}}\llbracket C^{2^{0}pt}\rrbracket\left(f\right)

(by
Lemma

7.4
, using that
C^{2^{0}pt}
is loop-free and that
f\in\mathcal{F}
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\sup_{0pt\in\mathbb{N}}\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C^{2^{0}pt}\rrbracket\left(f\right)

(by
Lemma

7.5
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(f\right)~.

∎

C.14.
Proof of
Lemma

8.3

See
8.3

Proof.

We show, by induction on the structure of
\mathsf{f}
, that
\llbracket\mathsf{f}\rrbracket
is locally bounded when viewed as a function of type
\llbracket\mathsf{f}\rrbracket\colon\mathbb{R}^{{\mathit{LV}^{\prime}}}\to\mathbb{R}
.

The case
\mathsf{f}=\mathsf{t}

\llbracket\mathsf{t}\rrbracket
is a piece-wise defined polynomial in finitely many variables, hence
\llbracket\mathsf{t}\rrbracket
is locally bounded.

The case
\mathsf{f}={\left[\upvarphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{f}

It is
\llbracket{\left[\upvarphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{f}\rrbracket\leq\llbracket\mathsf{f}\rrbracket
and
\llbracket\mathsf{f}\rrbracket
is locally bounded by the I.H.
Hence
\llbracket{\left[\upvarphi\right]}\cdot\mathsf{f}\rrbracket
is locally bounded, too.

The case
\mathsf{f}=q\cdot\mathsf{g}

By the I.H.,
\mathsf{g}
is locally bounded, i.e., for every
K\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{\mathit{LV}^{\prime}}=\mathbb{R}^{\mathit{LV}^{\prime}}
we have
\sup_{\sigma\in K}|\llbracket\mathsf{g}\rrbracket|<\infty
.
But then we also have
\sup_{\sigma\in K}|\llbracket q\cdot\mathsf{g}\rrbracket|=\sup_{\sigma\in K}|q\cdot\llbracket\mathsf{g}\rrbracket|=\sup_{\sigma\in K}|q|\cdot|\llbracket\mathsf{g}\rrbracket|=\sup_{\sigma\in K}q\cdot|\llbracket\mathsf{g}\rrbracket|=q\cdot\sup_{\sigma\in K}|\llbracket\mathsf{g}\rrbracket|<\infty
.

The case
\mathsf{f}=\mathsf{g}+\mathsf{h}

Similar to the previous case, using I.H. for both
\mathsf{g}
and
\mathsf{h}
.

The case
\mathsf{f}={\reflectbox{{{S}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\!_{[a,b]}\,x\colon}\mathsf{g}

Let
K\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{\mathit{LV}^{\prime}}
be compact.
Let
K^{\prime}
be the (compact) cross product of
K
and
{[a,b]}
, i.e.,
K^{\prime}=\{\sigma[x\mapsto\xi]\in\mathbb{R}^{{\mathit{LV}^{\prime}}\cup\{x\}}\mid\sigma\in K,\xi\in{[a,b]}\}
.
We have to show that
\sup_{\sigma\in K}|\llbracket f\rrbracket(\sigma)|<\infty
.
We argue as follows:

\displaystyle\sup_{\sigma\in K}\left|\llbracket f\rrbracket(\sigma)\right|

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\sup_{\sigma\in K}\left|\llbracket{\reflectbox{{{S}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\!_{[a,b]}\,x\colon}\mathsf{g}\rrbracket(\sigma)\right|

(definition of
\llbracket{\reflectbox{{{S}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\!_{[a,b]}\,x\colon}\mathsf{g}\rrbracket
)

\displaystyle~{}={}~

\displaystyle\sup_{\sigma\in K}\left|\sup_{\xi\in{[a,b]}}\llbracket\mathsf{g}\rrbracket(\sigma[x\mapsto\xi])\right|

(absolute value of
\sup

\leq

\sup
of absolute value)

\displaystyle~{}\leq{}~

\displaystyle\sup_{\sigma\in K}\sup_{\xi\in{[a,b]}}\left|\llbracket\mathsf{g}\rrbracket(\sigma[x\mapsto\xi])\right|

(because
K^{\prime}=\{\sigma[x\mapsto\xi]\mid\sigma\in K,\xi\in{[a,b]}\}
)

\displaystyle~{}\leq{}~

\displaystyle\sup_{\sigma\in K^{\prime}}\left|\llbracket\mathsf{g}\rrbracket(\sigma)\right|

(
\llbracket\mathsf{g}\rrbracket
locally bounded by I.H.)

\displaystyle~{}<{}~

\displaystyle\infty

The case
\mathsf{f}={\raisebox{0.6pt}{\rotatebox{-30.0}{{{\reflectbox{J}}}}\hskip-0.43057pt}\!_{[a,b]}\,x\colon}\mathsf{g}

This case is analogous to the

S

-case noticing that, for every set
A\subseteq\mathbb{R}
, we have
|\inf A|=|-\sup-A|=|\sup-A|\leq\sup|-A|=\sup|A|
, where
-A=\{-a\mid a\in A\}
.

∎

C.15.
Proof of
Lemma

8.5

See
8.5

Proof.

By induction on the structure of
\mathsf{f}
.
The crucial observation is that infima and suprema can be expressed in FO.
In all of the following we assume that
y\notin{\mathsf{free}(\mathsf{f})}\subseteq\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\}
.

The case
\mathsf{f}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})=\mathsf{t}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})

We first define
\uppsi_{\mathsf{t}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},y)
by induction on the structure of the syntactic term
\mathsf{t}
.

•

\mathsf{t}=q
.
We define

\uppsi_{\mathsf{t}}(y)\quad\text{as}\quad q=y~.

•

\mathsf{t}=x
.
We define

\uppsi_{\mathsf{t}}(x,y)\quad\text{as}\quad x=y~.

•

\mathsf{t}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})=\mathsf{u}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})+\mathsf{v}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})
.
We define

\uppsi_{\mathsf{t}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},y)\quad\text{as}\quad\exists y_{1}\colon\exists y_{2}\colon y=y_{1}+y_{2}\land\uppsi_{\mathsf{u}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},y_{1})\land\uppsi_{\mathsf{v}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},y_{2})~.

•

\mathsf{t}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})=\mathsf{u}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\cdot\mathsf{v}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})
. Similar to the previous case.

•

\mathsf{t}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})=\mathsf{u}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\mathbin{\dot{-}}\mathsf{v}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})
.
This case is the most interesting one due to monus.
We define
\uppsi_{\mathsf{t}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},y)
as

\displaystyle\exists y_{1}\colon\exists y_{2}\colon

\displaystyle\uppsi_{\mathsf{u}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},y_{1})\land\uppsi_{\mathsf{v}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},y_{2})

\displaystyle\land\left(y_{1}\geq y_{2}{}\rightarrow{}y=y_{1}-y_{2}\right)\land\left(y_{1}<y_{2}{}\rightarrow{}y=0\right)~.

Now that we have defined
\uppsi_{\mathsf{t}}
, we define

\uppsi_{\mathsf{f}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},y)\quad\text{as}\quad\mathsf{t}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})=y~.

The case
\mathsf{f}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})={\left[\upvarphi(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\right]}\cdot\mathsf{g}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})

We first define an FO formula
\uppsi_{\upvarphi}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},b)
by induction on the structure of the syntactic guard
\upvarphi
.
The idea is to encode the (Boolean) result that
\upvarphi
evaluates to as a
\{0,1\}
-valued FO variable
b
.

•

\upvarphi(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})=\mathsf{t}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})<\mathsf{u}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})
.
We consider
\uppsi_{\mathsf{t}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},y)
and
\uppsi_{\mathsf{u}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},y)
as defined above and define
\uppsi_{\upvarphi}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},b)
as

\displaystyle\exists y_{1}\colon\exists y_{2}\colon

\displaystyle\uppsi_{\mathsf{u}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},y_{1})\land\uppsi_{\mathsf{v}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},y_{2})

\displaystyle\land\left(y_{1}<y_{2}{}\rightarrow{}b=1\right)\land\left(y_{1}\geq y_{2}{}\rightarrow{}b=0\right)~.

•

\upvarphi(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})=\upvarphi_{1}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\land\upvarphi_{2}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})
.
We consider
\uppsi_{\upvarphi_{1}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},b)
and
\uppsi_{\upvarphi_{2}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},b)
and define
\uppsi_{\upvarphi}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},b)
as

\displaystyle\exists b_{1}\colon\exists b_{2}\colon

\displaystyle\uppsi_{\upvarphi_{1}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},b_{1})\land\uppsi_{\upvarphi_{2}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},b_{2})

\displaystyle\land\left(b_{1}+b_{1}=2{}\rightarrow{}b=1\right)\land\left(b_{1}+b_{1}\neq 2{}\rightarrow{}b=0\right)~.

•

\upvarphi(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})=\neg\upvarphi^{\prime}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})
.
We consider
\uppsi_{\upvarphi^{\prime}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},b)
and define
\uppsi_{\upvarphi}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},b)
as

\displaystyle\exists b^{\prime}\colon\uppsi_{\upvarphi^{\prime}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},b^{\prime})\land b=1-b^{\prime}~.

Now that we have defined
\uppsi_{\upvarphi}
, we define

\uppsi_{\mathsf{f}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},y)\quad\text{as}\quad\exists b\colon\uppsi_{\upvarphi}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},b)\land(b=1{}\rightarrow{}\uppsi_{\mathsf{f}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},y))\land(b=0{}\rightarrow{}y=0)

The case
\mathsf{f}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})=q\cdot\mathsf{g}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})

We define

\uppsi_{\mathsf{f}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},y)\quad\text{as}\quad\exists y^{\prime}\colon y=q\cdot y^{\prime}\land\uppsi_{\mathsf{g}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},y^{\prime})~.

The case
\mathsf{f}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})=\mathsf{g}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})+\mathsf{h}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})

We define

\uppsi_{\mathsf{f}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},y)\quad\text{as}\quad\exists y_{1}\colon\exists y_{2}\colon y=y_{1}+y_{2}\land\uppsi_{\mathsf{g}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},y_{1})\land\uppsi_{\mathsf{h}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},y_{2})~.

The case
\mathsf{f}={\reflectbox{{{S}}}\hskip 0.86108pt\!_{[a,b]}\,x\colon}\mathsf{g}

To give an intuition about how we handle this case, we recall the standard definition of the supremum:
Let
A\subseteq\mathbb{R}
and
y\in\mathbb{R}
.
Then

\displaystyle y=\sup A

(
y
is an upper bound)

\displaystyle~{}\iff{}~

\displaystyle\text{for all }a\in A\colon a\leq y

(
y
is not greater than any upper bound)

\displaystyle\text{and for all }y^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}\colon(\text{for all }a\in A\colon a\leq y^{\prime})\implies y\leq y^{\prime}~.

We now construct FO formulae corresponding to the above two conjuncts.
Consider the formula
\uppsi_{\mathsf{g}}(x,x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},y)
obtained by applying the I.H. to
\mathsf{g}(x,x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})
.
We define

\uppsi_{\mathsf{g}}^{ub}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},u)\quad\text{as}\quad\forall a\leq\xi\leq b\colon\forall y\colon\uppsi_{\mathsf{g}}(\xi,x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},y){}\rightarrow{}y\leq u~.

We have for all
\sigma\in\mathbb{R}^{{\mathsf{free}(\mathsf{g})}}
and
r\in\mathbb{R}
that
\sigma,r\models\uppsi_{\mathsf{g}}^{ub}
iff for all
\xi\in{[a,b]}\colon\llbracket\mathsf{g}\rrbracket(\sigma[x\mapsto\xi])\leq r
.
Now we define

\uppsi_{\mathsf{f}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},y)\quad\text{as}\quad\uppsi_{\mathsf{g}}^{ub}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},y)\land\forall y^{\prime}\colon\uppsi_{\mathsf{g}}^{ub}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},y^{\prime}){}\rightarrow{}y\leq y^{\prime}

Then
\uppsi_{\mathsf{f}}
has the desired property.

The case
\mathsf{f}={\raisebox{0.6pt}{\rotatebox{-30.0}{{{\reflectbox{J}}}}\hskip-0.43057pt}\!_{[a,b]}\,x\colon}\mathsf{g}

Analogous to the previous case.

Finally, observe that for
quantifier-free

\mathsf{f}
(i.e., without

S

and

J

), the above inductive construction of
\uppsi_{\mathsf{f}}
can be done in linear time in the size of
\mathsf{f}
.
The so-obtained
\uppsi_{\mathsf{f}}
is not yet necessarily in existential prenex form, but it can be translated to the latter (again in linear time) by succesively applying the following standard prenexing rules which are true for all FO formulae
\uppsi
and
\uptheta
:

\displaystyle\uppsi{}\rightarrow{}(\exists x\colon\uptheta)

\displaystyle\text{ is equivalent to }\exists x\colon(\uppsi{}\rightarrow{}\uptheta)

\displaystyle\uppsi\land(\exists x\colon\uptheta)

\displaystyle\text{ is equivalent to }\exists x\colon(\uppsi\land\uptheta)

This completes the proof.
∎

C.16.
Proof of
Theorem

8.7

See
8.7

Proof.

Let
\sigma\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{\mathit{LV}^{\prime}}
be arbitrary and
consider the function
f_{\sigma}=\lambda\xi.{\left[\xi\geq 0\right]}\cdot\llbracket\mathsf{f}\rrbracket(\sigma[x\mapsto\xi])
of type
\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
.
By
Lemma

8.3
,
f_{\sigma}
is locally bounded.
By
Lemma

8.5
,
f_{\sigma}
is semi-algebraic.
It thus follows with
Lemma

8.6
that
f_{\sigma}
is Riemann-integrable on
{[a,b]}
.
Hence, since
\sigma
was arbitrary, we may conclude that
\llbracket\mathsf{f}\rrbracket
is Riemann-integrable on
{[a,b]}
w.r.t.
x
.
∎

C.17.
Proof of
Lemma

8.9

See
8.9

Proof.

The fact that
\llbracket\mathsf{f}\rrbracket\sqsubseteq\llbracket\mathsf{g}\rrbracket
iff the FO formula in (
4
) is valid follows directly from
Lemma

8.5
.
However, note that the formula in (
4
) is not necessarily quantifier-free as
\uppsi_{\mathsf{f}^{\prime}}
and
\uppsi_{\mathsf{g}^{\prime}}
may contain an
\exists
-prefix (see
Lemma

8.5
).
By applying the standard prenexing rules

\displaystyle(\exists x\colon\uppsi)\land\uptheta

\displaystyle\quad\text{is equivalent to}\quad(\exists x\colon\uppsi\land\uptheta)

\displaystyle\text{and}\qquad(\exists x\colon\uppsi){}\rightarrow{}\uptheta

\displaystyle\quad\text{is equivalent to}\quad\forall x\colon(\uppsi{}\rightarrow{}\uptheta)

which hold for all FO formulae
\uppsi
and
\uptheta
with
x\notin{\mathsf{free}(\uptheta)}
, we can transform the formula in (
4
) to universal prenex form.
Checking validity of the latter is equivalent to checking validity of the
quantifier-free
formula obtained by dropping the
\forall
-prefix.
∎

C.18.
Substitution Lemma

Lemma C.5 (Substitution Lemma
(
Batz et al., 2021
;
Schröer et al., 2023
)
).

Let
\mathsf{f}\in\mathsf{Expr}
such that
x\in{\mathsf{free}(\mathsf{f})}
and let
\mathsf{t}\in\mathsf{Terms}
be a syntactic term.
Consider the expression
\mathsf{f}[x/\mathsf{t}]\in\mathsf{Expr}
obtained by (syntactically) substituting every free occurrence of
x
in
\mathsf{f}
by
\mathsf{t}
in a capture-avoiding manner (i.e., by possibly renaming variables appropriately).
Then

\llbracket\mathsf{f}[x/\mathsf{t}]\rrbracket~{}={}~\llbracket\mathsf{f}\rrbracket[x/\llbracket\mathsf{t}\rrbracket]{\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}~{}={}~\lambda\sigma.\llbracket\mathsf{f}\rrbracket(\sigma[x\mapsto\llbracket\mathsf{t}\rrbracket(\sigma)])}~.

C.19.
Proof of
Theorem

8.12

See
8.12

Proof.

Recall
Definition

7.3
.
By
Theorem

8.7
, all
\llbracket\mathsf{f}\rrbracket
are bounded and Riemann integrable on
{{[0,1]}}
w.r.t. every
\mathtt{x}\in V
.
The arithmetic expressions representable by
\mathsf{Terms}
, i.e., the functions
\llbracket\mathsf{t}\rrbracket
for
\mathsf{t}\in\mathsf{Terms}
, are locally bounded since they are piecewise defined polynomials.
The closure properties from
Definition

7.3
all follow immediately from the definition of
\mathsf{Expr}
, except the substitution
\llbracket\mathsf{f}\rrbracket[\mathtt{x}/E]
.
Here, the crucial observation is that
semantic substitution corresponds to syntactic substitution
, see
Lemma

C.5
.
∎

C.20.
Computing Riemann
\mathsf{w}(\mathsf{l})\mathsf{p}
Syntactically

Lemma C.6 (Computing Riemann
\mathsf{w}(\mathsf{l})\mathsf{p}
Syntactically).

For all
C\in\mathsf{pWhile}(\mathsf{Terms},\mathsf{Guards})
,
C
loop-free, and integers
N\geq 1
we can effectively do the following:
For every given …

(1)

…
\mathsf{f}\in\mathsf{Expr}
, compute
\mathsf{g}\in\mathsf{Expr}
such that
\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\llbracket\mathsf{f}\rrbracket\right)=\llbracket\mathsf{g}\rrbracket
.

(2)

…
\mathsf{f}\in\mathsf{Expr}
, compute
\mathsf{g}\in\mathsf{Expr}
such that
\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\llbracket\mathsf{f}\rrbracket\right)=\llbracket\mathsf{g}\rrbracket
.

(3)

…
\mathsf{f}\in\mathsf{Expr}^{\leq 1}
, compute
\mathsf{g}\in\mathsf{Expr}^{\leq 1}
such that
\underline{\mathsf{wl}}\mathsf{p}^{N}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\llbracket\mathsf{f}\rrbracket\right)=\llbracket\mathsf{g}\rrbracket
.

(4)

…
\mathsf{f}\in\mathsf{Expr}^{\leq 1}
, compute
\mathsf{g}\in\mathsf{Expr}^{\leq 1}
such that
\overline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{l}\overline{\mathsf{p}}^{N}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\llbracket\mathsf{f}\rrbracket\right)=\llbracket\mathsf{g}\rrbracket
.

In (1) and (3), if
\mathsf{f}
is

S

-free, then so is
\mathsf{g}
.
In (2) and (4), if
\mathsf{f}
is

J

-free, then so is
\mathsf{g}
.

Proof.

By induction on the structure of
C
.
Most cases are immediate as we have essentially designed
\mathsf{Expr}
such that
\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{Expr}}
(
\mathbb{E}^{\leq 1}_{\mathsf{Expr}}
) is closed under
\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{N}\llbracket C\rrbracket
and
\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}\llbracket C\rrbracket
(
\underline{\mathsf{wl}}\mathsf{p}^{N}\llbracket C\rrbracket
and
\overline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{l}\overline{\mathsf{p}}^{N}\llbracket C\rrbracket
, resp.) for loop-free
C
.
For the assignment
\mathtt{x}\coloneq E
we rely on syntactic substitution (
Lemma

C.5
).
∎

C.21.
Proof of
Theorem

8.13

See
8.13

Proof.

By computing a syntactic representation of the corresponding Riemann
\mathsf{w}(\mathsf{l})\mathsf{p}
as in
Lemma

C.6
, translating to PNF via
Lemma

8.8
, and applying the quantitative entailment check from
Lemma

8.9
.
The
gray
implications follow from soundness of the Riemann
\mathsf{w}(\mathsf{l})\mathsf{p}
(
Lemma

5.5
).
∎

C.22.
Proof of
Theorem

8.14

See
8.14

Proof.

Since
B
is loop-free we can apply
Lemma

C.6
to compute
i\in\mathsf{Expr}
(resp.
j\in\mathsf{Expr}^{\leq 1}
) such that
\llbracket i\rrbracket=\overline{\mathsf{wp}}^{N}\llbracket B\rrbracket\left(\llbracket I\rrbracket\right)
(resp.
\llbracket j\rrbracket=\underline{\mathsf{wl}}\mathsf{p}^{N}\llbracket B\rrbracket\left(\llbracket J\rrbracket\right)
) with
i
is

J

-free (resp

S

-free). Then, applying
Lemma

6.1
, we need to check that

[\varphi]\cdot\llbracket\mathsf{f}\rrbracket+[\neg\varphi]\cdot\llbracket i\rrbracket\leq\llbracket I\rrbracket
(resp.
\llbracket J\rrbracket\leq[\varphi]\cdot\llbracket\mathsf{f}\rrbracket+[\neg\varphi]\cdot\llbracket j\rrbracket
). By
Theorem

8.13
this inequality can be reduced to
\mathsf{QF\_NRA}
.
∎

C.23.
Proof of
Theorem

8.15

See
8.15

Proof.

We show membership in
\mathsf{coRE}
first (which is the more interesting direction).
Consider the complement of the first problem:

(complement of problem (
1
))

\displaystyle\text{``Does there exist }\sigma\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{V}\text{ s.t.\ }\llbracket\mathsf{g}\rrbracket(\sigma)<\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\llbracket\mathsf{f}\rrbracket\right)(\sigma)\text{ ?''}

If the answer to the above question is
yes
, then, since

\sup_{n\geq 1}\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{n}\llbracket C^{n}\rrbracket\left(\llbracket\mathsf{f}\rrbracket\right)=\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\llbracket\mathsf{f}\rrbracket\right)

by
Theorem

7.6
, there must exist
\sigma\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{V}
and
n\geq 1
such that
\llbracket\mathsf{g}\rrbracket(\sigma)<\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{n}\llbracket C^{n}\rrbracket\left(\llbracket\mathsf{f}\rrbracket\right)(\sigma)
.
Hence our semi-decision procedure will check the latter inequality for increasing values of
n
.
To do so for a fixed
n
, the procedure constructs
\mathsf{h}
such that
\llbracket\mathsf{h}\rrbracket=\underline{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{p}^{n}\llbracket C^{n}\rrbracket\left(\llbracket\mathsf{f}\rrbracket\right)
as in
Lemma

C.6
and then invokes
Lemma

8.5
to obtain FO formulae
\uppsi_{\mathsf{g}}({\mathsf{free}(\mathsf{g})},y_{\mathsf{g}})
and
\uppsi_{\mathsf{h}}({\mathsf{free}(\mathsf{h})},y_{\mathsf{h}})
encoding
\mathsf{g}
and
\mathsf{h}
.
It then only remains to check if the FO formula
(\uppsi_{\mathsf{g}}\land\uppsi_{\mathsf{h}}){}\rightarrow{}y_{\mathsf{g}}<y_{\mathsf{h}}
is satisfiable, which is decidable.

To see that the complement of problem (
1
) is
\mathsf{RE}
-hard we reduce the halting problem to it.
We proceed as in
(
Kaminski and Katoen, 2015
)
.
The standard halting problem is equivalent to checking if
\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{std}\rrbracket\left(1\right)(\sigma)=1
where
C_{std}\in\mathsf{pWhile}(\mathsf{Terms},\mathsf{Guards})
is a given standard program, i.e., it does not contain any probabilistic constructs, and
\sigma\in\mathbb{Q}^{V}
is a given initial state.
Now consider

C~{}={}~\{\mathtt{x}_{1}\coloneq\sigma(\mathtt{x}_{1})\,;\,\ldots\,;\,\mathtt{x}_{n}\coloneq\sigma(\mathtt{x}_{n})\,;\,C_{std}\}~[\tfrac{1}{2}]~\{\mathtt{skip}\}

where
\{\mathtt{x}_{1},\ldots,\mathtt{x}_{n}\}=V
.
Then
\tfrac{1}{2}<\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(1\right)
iff
\mathsf{wp}\llbracket C_{std}\rrbracket\left(1\right)(\sigma)>0
iff
C_{std}
terminates on input
\sigma
.

The argument for
\mathsf{coRE}
membership of problem (
2
) is completely analogous.
For
\mathsf{coRE}
-hardness we argue dually (we define
C
as above):

\mathsf{wlp}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(0\right)<\tfrac{1}{2}
iff
\mathsf{wlp}\llbracket C_{std}\rrbracket\left(0\right)(\sigma)<\tfrac{1}{2}
iff
C_{std}
terminates on input
\sigma
.
The latter argument works because for standard programs we have
\mathsf{wlp}\llbracket C_{std}\rrbracket\left(0\right)(\sigma)=0
iff
C_{std}
terminates on
\sigma
, and
\mathsf{wlp}\llbracket C_{std}\rrbracket\left(0\right)(\sigma)=1
iff
C_{std}
does not terminate on
\sigma
.
∎
